# Changelog

The history of GitHub Actions Monitor, from the first version to the latest. Each version groups its changes under Added, Changed, Fixed and Security.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **A wall screen or a kiosk that stays on for days reloads itself when the monitor is updated or restarted**: the new route `/api/version` (never cached, with the same access as the other reads) answers `{"version", "started"}`, and the wall screens and the kiosk profile ask it every `screens.version_check_seconds` seconds (300 by default; 0 = never; the least is 30). When the version or the start time is not the one the page loaded, it reloads itself, so a TV never keeps an old page. It reloads only after a valid answer that differs: a monitor that does not answer never makes it reload in a loop. The guides of the screens, the kiosk and the API say so in the eight languages.

- **A result that is too old is called stale** (`ci.stale_result_hours`, 0 = off by default; 72 is a good value): a workflow whose last result is older than that many hours is marked `stale_result` in the lists of workflows of `/api/data` and shows the label *stale* in the table of the CI sentinel. It is information only: a stale result is neither red nor green, changes nothing in the score, and a repository whose runs could not be read this time is not marked. It reuses the rule of the aging of the reds (`redrule.py`). The guide of the sentinel says so in the eight languages.

- **A countdown to the 60 days after which GitHub switches off the scheduled workflows** (`sentinel.schedule_countdown_after_days`, 45 by default, 1 to 59): for a public repository with a scheduled workflow whose last push is older than that, the hygiene panel of the sentinel adds an information row with the days left. It uses only the date of the last push that the sentinel already reads (no new call to GitHub), says *not evaluable* when that date is not known, ignores private repositories and is never red. The guide of the sentinel says so in the eight languages.

- **Density, less movement and a palette for colour blindness for the whole page** (`display.density`, `display.reduce_motion`, `display.palette`; `?density=`, `?palette=` for one window): `dense` tightens the spacing and the type of every tab, not only of Now; `reduce_motion: on` stops every animation and transition whatever the system says; the `colorblind` palette (blue, vermilion, yellow and a lighter blue, one set for each mode) keeps the four states apart for the three common kinds of colour blindness, and the state is always written in words and shape too.

- **A contrast gate** (`tools/check_contrast.py`, run by the CI): every text on every surface of the dark and light modes and of the sixteen themes reaches 4.5:1, the colour of a state reaches 3:1, the colour-blind palette is checked with a simulation of the three kinds of colour blindness, and a new dimming of text with `opacity` fails the build.

- **The guides and the next step on the command line**: `monitor_cli docs list` names the guides of the installed version with their title and `docs show NAME` prints one in the language of `--lang` (the English text when a guide is not translated); it reads the installation, so it needs no running monitor. Every error of the command now carries a `help`: one sentence with the next step, often naming the guide to read; in text it is the last line (`help: ...`), with `--json` it is the field `help` next to `error`.

- **The page does not jump while you read** (`display.live_throttle_s`, 30 by default, 0 turns it off): while the pointer or the keyboard focus is inside the tab you are reading, the periodic refresh of the data waits and is applied when you leave, never more often than that many seconds; a refresh you ask for is applied at once.

- **What is new after an update** (`display.whats_new`, on): a notice that never interrupts offers the entries of the changelog of the versions you skipped, in your language, read from the installed version (`GET /api/whats-new`); what you have seen is kept in the browser before anything is shown, and the palette opens it at any time.

- **A glossary and the limits of the analysis** (`GET /api/glossary`; `report_export.limits`, on): the palette opens a window with what each figure means exactly (red on main, success rate, streak, duration, wait in the queue, recent failure rate), written with the real parameters of the calculation and of your settings, the attacks the rules of the linter look for, six places to learn more and the limits of the analysis, which the saved and printed reports now end with.

- **Branches to watch, repository by repository, and a count of what is left out** (`ci.branches_by_repo`, entries `owner/repo=pattern`, empty by default): a maintenance branch such as `release-*` can be watched in one repository without watching it in all (`ci.extra_branches` keeps adding to every repository). The failures on branches that nobody asked to watch no longer vanish: the Now tab says how many there are (they still do not count as red) and lists the repository, workflow and branch of each.

- **The header filter says what it covers, the selector of repositories says what is happening, and an empty or failed page says what to do**: a note lists the tabs that follow the filter and the ones that are always global; the selector of repositories shows loading, failed with a button to try again, or nothing yet; the error bar has a button to try again, and a page with no repositories or with no open alerts shows a box with the next step.

- **Every red says what started it** (pull request, push to main, scheduled, manual, merge queue, chained workflow): the class travels with the red rows, with the failing runs of `/api/data` (`cls`) and with the `ci.red` alerts, which show it as a small tag and can be filtered by it in the Alerts tab ("Started by").

- **Expected red, with a reason, an owner and an end** (`expected_red.enabled`, off by default; `expected_red.max_days`, 30): a workflow that is red on purpose for a while (a pipeline under construction) is marked from the Alerts tab or the palette; while the mark lasts its red is listed apart, is not counted and does not alert, and when it runs out the red comes back and one alert says so. It is not a silence by pattern: it names one workflow (or `*` for a whole repository), it needs a reason and an owner and it can never last longer than the maximum. `GET` and `POST /api/expected-red`; a note of this monitor, never a change on GitHub.

- **The duration of a run in progress counts up on the page, and an empty list says what to do next**: the time a run and its jobs have been running advances every second between two refreshes, with no request to the monitor (and not while the tab is hidden). The lists that only said "No data." or "No results." (charts, history, team, the repositories read in each cycle, reports, updates) now add a sentence with the cause and the next step, with a button to refresh or to open Settings or Repositories. The smoke test of the page checks both in a real browser.

- **A warning about abnormal clones by user** (`audit_feed.clone_alert.enabled`, off by default; `threshold`, `window_hours`, `check_minutes`, `bot_patterns`): it reads the audit log of the organization (the git events, which only some plans offer) at most every `check_minutes` minutes and names the users who cloned more different repositories than the threshold in the window, leaving out robots by name; the alert names the user and the repositories, never an address. `GET /api/clone-anomalies`.

- **Metadata columns in the Repositories table** (`fleet.meta_columns`, empty by default; `fleet.meta_per_call`, `fleet.meta_ttl_hours`): up to five columns whose value is one line of a file of each repository, found by a path and a regular expression (a template version, the pin of an SDK). The files are read with conditional reads, the value is kept by the SHA of the file, a few files are read per round, a missing file is a dash and a regular expression that could take exponential time is refused. `GET /api/meta-columns`.

- **People from outside the team who got no answer** (`sentinels.collab.rules.external_days`, `sentinels.collab.rules.team_members`): with the watch of outsiders on, an issue or a pull request of someone outside the team that nobody has answered after the chosen number of days is an alert (until now only issues, after one day); the people of the team list are never chased and only a flag is kept, never their name.

- **The page on a phone and the wall screens are measured in a real browser**: `python tools/check_mobile.py` (a window of 375 px, the compact view, every tab) fails on a control under 44 x 44 px, on a page that scrolls sideways, on a menu that is not a row or on dialogs that ignore the on-screen keyboard; the About and Help buttons of the side bar, the only ones under it, now keep a finger's width. `python tools/check_screens.py` also measures the contrast of every text of the wall screens against the colour (or gradient) behind it, in each theme (`--theme NAME ...`), and fails under 4.5:1 (3:1 for large type): the muted text of the sixteen themes is lifted on the wall screens, and the "more failures" mark of the glance strip keeps its state in its border and its sign instead of in the colour of its text.

- **The monitor can be installed as an app on a phone, a tablet or a computer** (`pwa.enabled`, off by default; `pwa.refresh_on_return`, on): the page offers a manifest, icons and a small service worker that keeps only an offline page and the icons, never the page or any data, and when the app comes back to the front or the network returns the page asks for fresh data at once. The files are served behind the access token of the network mode.

- **A compact view for a phone** (`ui.compact`, `auto` by default; `?compact=1` or `?compact=0` for one window): in a narrow window the menu goes to the top as a row of tabs, the spacing tightens, the tables scroll sideways, a touch screen gets targets of at least 44 pixels and the dialogs follow the height left by the on-screen keyboard.

- **Phones and tablets can be paired with a one-use QR code** (`pairing.enabled`, off by default and only in the network mode; `pairing.code_minutes`, `pairing.max_devices`, `pairing.device_days`): a window shows a QR code, drawn by the monitor itself, with a code that works once; the phone gets a token of its own, not the access token, and can be taken away alone in the same window. Only the hash of the token is kept, a wrong code counts against the address and every pairing and every revocation leaves a line in the log of actions.

- **A palette on Ctrl+K and keyboard shortcuts** (`ui.palette`, on by default): a search box that accepts any part of the words jumps to a tab or a repository, refreshes, changes the theme, opens the help or pairs a phone; `?` lists the shortcuts, `/` goes to the search box of the tab, `r` refreshes and `[` `]` change tab.

- **A safe margin for a television that cuts the border (overscan)** (`screens.safe_margin_percent` and `kiosk.safe_margin_percent`, 0 by default, 0 to 10; `?safe=` for one window): the wall screens draw everything smaller inside that part of the window and the kiosk keeps its page that far from each edge, so the clock and the border of the cards are not cut. The guides of the screens and of the kiosk say how to choose it.
- **The type of the kiosk can grow with the window** (`kiosk.scale_with_window`, off by default; `?fit=1` or `?fit=0` for one window): the type size of the kiosk is the one of a window of 1080 px on its short side, and with this on it grows with the window, so a 4K television, an ultra-wide monitor or a turned screen shows the same page as a Full HD one instead of a page with tiny type.
- **`tools/check_screens.py` measures the wall screens and the kiosk with the DOM**: every screen in the eight languages, in seven window sizes (1920x1080, 2560x1440, 3840x2160, the ultra-wide 3440x1440 and 5120x1440, and turned 1080x1920 and 2160x3840) and with data that stress it (repository names of 90 characters, German compound words, Japanese names without spaces, the most rows the monitor can send, figures of seven digits, no data, a single datum, an old reading). It reports a page that scrolls, a text or box beyond the edge or the safe margin, content that does not fit, a heading that ends in dots, two texts that cover each other, a type below a hundredth of the short side of the window and rows hidden without notice, and it writes every finding as JSON. A browser test measures a few cells in the normal suite and the whole matrix in the CI.
- **A list that cannot show every row says how many it left out** ("+3 more", in the eight languages): the events, the queue, the failures, the heat map and the map of groups keep the most important rows and end in that line instead of dropping rows without a word.

- **Save the report of a tab through the Save as window of the browser**: the button at the top of the page opens a window with the boxes TXT (marked), PDF (marked) and JSON (optional, not marked) and, once accepted, the Save as window of the browser for each format, with the suggested name `monitor-<tab>-<date>.<extension>` and the right file type; a browser without that window downloads the file and says where it went. The PDF is written by the monitor itself with no library and the standard Helvetica font (`GET /api/report/tab.pdf`, this computer only, recorded in the log of actions); Japanese and Chinese, and the choice "The browser" in the window, use the print window of the browser instead, and a PDF with broken characters is never written.

- **Polling that knows whether anybody is looking** (`modes.idle_minutes`, 0 = off by default; 10 is a good start): when no page, wall screen or live stream has asked the monitor for data for that many minutes, the refresh cycle stretches to the cold interval of the mode in force until somebody looks again, and a hidden tab stops asking while it is hidden. It never goes cold while an alert can leave the computer (a channel of notices, the webhook receiver, the feed for an AI, the chat or the beep of this computer), and the sentinels keep their own clocks. `/api/health` says whether it is cold and what keeps it at its pace.

- **A Fleet wall screen of its own** (`/screen/fleet`, the second of four: Overview, Fleet, Right now, Sentinels): every repository of the fleet by group, in the grouping and order of the Fleet tab, as a small square for each healthy one and a chip with the name for each one that needs a look, with the totals on top. What does not fit is counted ("+3 more"), a hidden repository is named as on the other screens, and it has its own theme (`screens.theme_fleet`), logo (`screens.logo_fleet`), entry in the Screens menu and place in the multi-monitor set.

- **The wall screens have life** (`screens.life`, `screens.glance_seconds`): a figure that changes between two updates flashes where it changed, a new failure sends a wave over its box, a change of state leaves a short trail, and a few chips in the header say what changed ("Red +1", "Running +2") for a few seconds. Nothing blinks more than twice a second and nothing moves with the system setting for less motion.

- **The wall screens can show only what needs a look and rest when it is quiet** (`screens.hide_healthy`, `screens.idle_dim_minutes`, `screens.transition`; all off or as before by default): the healthy repositories, the groups without red or yellow and the quiet rows of the heat map can be left out, a screen with nothing new for a while dims until the next change, and the change from one screen to the next fades, slides or cuts.

- **A smallest letter for the wall screens** (`screens.min_font_px`, 12 pixels by default, 8 to 32): in a small window the screens keep the letter and show fewer rows, saying how many they left out ("+3 more"), instead of shrinking the text. `tools/check_screens.py` now measures the Fleet screen and the smallest letter in pixels as well.

- **Print the report of a tab**: the print button shows the same clean report as the PDF (title with the name of the tab, date and time, version of the monitor, main figures, alerts and the tables of the tab, with no menus or bars) and opens the print window; the text file, the data file, the PDF and the printed page are all made from ONE report served by `GET /api/report/tab`, for every tab and in the eight languages.

- **Settings of the report**: `report_export.txt`, `report_export.pdf` and `report_export.json` choose which boxes start marked, `report_export.name` the suggested file name, `report_export.paper` the paper of the PDF (A4 or Letter) and `report_export.pdf_path` the way the PDF is made (automatic, the monitor or the browser).

- **A test proves that the sentinels need no interface**: in a fresh process that never loads the page, the web server or any module of the interface, every sentinel that is on starts its own thread, reads through the reader it was given and completes a cycle, and a sentinel that is off never runs.

- **Exit codes of `status` for an operator** (`--status-codes operator` or the setting `cli.status_codes`; `classic` by default, so no script changes): 0 healthy, 1 nothing serving (the monitor cannot be reached or refuses the credential) and 2 up but needs attention (offline, no data yet, blind credential, the refresh paused by the allowance, an error, or code on the disk of another version than the running process). `/api/health` now says `code_stale`, `status` prints the reasons (`attention` in `--json`) and `--doctor` has a `running` check for a monitor that was not restarted after an update.
- **`doctor --latency`**: measures, with reads only, how late the events of the account tell a push compared with the run of that very commit (median and worst of the latest pushes, plus the round trip), and what the worst wait of the free change detector would be with your `events_feed.interval_seconds`. Nothing is written on GitHub and no test run is created; a read that fails is reported as not evaluable, with its cause, never as a defect.
- **`monitor N` and the operator codes in `watch status`**: `monitor_cli.py monitor [N]` is `watch status` that refreshes every N seconds (the floor and the default of `cli.watch` apply), a screen for a terminal left open. `watch status` now follows the operator codes (`--status-codes operator`) also when the read fails: a monitor that cannot be reached or refuses the credential is 1, an answer that cannot be read is 2.

- **`monitor_cli.py watch branch`**: `git push && monitor_cli.py watch branch` follows the workflows of the commit of the folder you are in (repository, branch and commit from git; `--repo`, `--branch`, `--single-pass`, `--wait`), waits for them to appear and finish and ends with 0 when all passed and 1 when one failed or did not finish in time (`cli.watch.branch_timeout_seconds`, 900 by default). It reads the runs the monitor already holds, spends no allowance and never prints the address of the remote.

- **Settings that cannot work together are named** (`conflicts` in `--doctor`, at the start, and refused with `config_check.strict`): a repository in both `repos` and `exclude`, `ecosystems` with a list of repositories, `updates.check` while air-gapped, sounds off with the beep, the wall screens, the ambience or the tick on, quiet hours that start and end at the same hour, AI actions that execute without being enabled, and a kiosk that rotates while it is off. Each message is in the eight languages.

- **Full screen, Screens and hot repositories next to save and print**: the buttons of the full screen, of the Screens menu and of the hot repositories filter now sit in the bar at the top of the page, to the left of the buttons that save and print the report, and keep their order; they stay hidden in the kiosk and in the all-repositories view as before.

- **What you fold in the Sentinel and in the lists of the page stays folded after a refresh**: the sections A1 to A13 of the CI sentinel were drawn again at every refresh with their default state because the page only listened to the folding of other tabs, so a section folded by hand opened again by itself. Every folding block now has a stable name and the page remembers the choice of each one, in memory and, with `ui.remember_folds` on (the default), in the browser too; the cards of the Collab, Security, Delivery and Supply chain sentinels, the lists of notices of the first tab and the versions of the updater use the same memory. Only a click of the person records a choice, so what the page opens by itself is never taken for one, and a browser test folds, forces a refresh of the data and checks that it stays folded in three languages.
- **An alert of a Sentinel panel keeps one identity whatever the language of the page**: the identity of an alert that comes from a panel (the incomplete releases of A10 among them) was made from the translated title of its section, so when the panels were built once for the cycle in one language and once for a request of the page in another, the same finding was closed and opened again in turns and the file of events `ai_alerts.jsonl` filled with `opened` and `resolved` lines for one cause. The identity is now made from the panel, the repository and the key of the finding, which never change with the language, a finding that stays open makes no new event at any cycle, and a test builds every panel in the eight languages and asks for the same identities. The alerts of the panels get a new identity once, when the monitor starts after the update.
- **The two AI indicators of the bar are icons instead of words**: the texts "Built-in AI" and "External AI" became two buttons with a drawing (a chip for the AI built into the monitor, waves for an external AI tool). A badge tells the state by its shape and not only by its colour (a check is on, a slash on a dashed drawing is off, a triangle is an error: the built-in AI is configured but does not answer), the name for screen readers and the tooltip give the exact state in the language of the page, the icon takes the focus and answers to Enter and Space, and a click opens the AI section of the Settings. They keep the size of the bar in every language and in both themes.
- **The About panel shows the logo of the monitor and, if you want, the one of your organization** (`ui.about_org_logo`, off by default, in the General tab): the drawing of the monitor is always at the top, and with the setting on the image you uploaded for the wall screens (This browser tab) is shown next to it with an alternative text in the language of the page. Both follow the width of the panel without deforming, and when no logo was uploaded or its file cannot be read a short note replaces the image and the panel keeps working.
- **The workflow linter can tell you about actions that are archived or no longer looked after** (rule `archived_action`, off by default because it asks GitHub; information only): it lists the actions and reusable workflows of GitHub that a workflow uses whose repository is archived and, if you also switch on `lint.maintenance.inactive`, those with no push for `lint.maintenance.inactive_years` years (2 by default). It asks GitHub once per action through the allowance already coordinated for the sentinel (at most `lint.maintenance.max_calls_per_tick` per scan, each answer kept for `lint.maintenance.refresh_seconds`), never goes above information level, never makes `--fail-on` fail, and without a network it judges nothing and says how many actions it could not read. From the command line: `--check-actions` and `--inactive-years N`.

- **The limit of a webhook body is a setting, and a corpus of hostile deliveries guards the receiver** (`webhook.max_body_kb`, 1024 by default, 1 to 25600): the receiver refused every body over 256 KB, so the big deliveries of GitHub (a push with many commits, a check suite) failed on its side. The limit is now yours to set (1 MiB by default, up to the 25 MiB that GitHub sends), a body over it is refused with `413` before it is read, and the refusals are counted apart in `stats.too_big` of `GET /api/live-status`. Tests send the receiver empty, null, mistyped, huge, unknown and repeated deliveries: it always answers with a code and never fails or waits.

- **A late delivery never takes a run back**: GitHub does not promise the order of deliveries, and a `queued` event that arrived after the `completed` one could show a finished run as waiting. A run or a job now only moves forward (queued, in progress, completed): an event that says less than what is already known about it, also from what the last cycle read, changes nothing and is counted in `stats.out_of_order` of `GET /api/live-status`. A new attempt of a run starts again. The tests feed every order of the three states.

- **The events of the webhook wait in a bounded queue, and `/readyz` says whether the monitor is fit to take work** (`webhook.queue_max`, 500 by default, 1 to 10000): an accepted event is applied by a worker, so GitHub is answered fast. When the queue is full the receiver answers `503` with `Retry-After` and forgets the delivery, so the one that GitHub sends again is accepted and an event is never dropped in silence. `GET /readyz` (this computer only) answers `200` or `503` with the check that fails (`loaded`, `cycle`, `store`, `queue`); `GET /api/health` still only says that the process lives. The depth of the queue and the refusals are in `GET /api/live-status`.

- **A run that a lost delivery left open is reconciled** (`webhook.stale_after_h`, 6 by default, 1 to 168): a run that an event left queued or in progress and that gets no final event in that time is marked `stale` in the run list and its repository is read again in the next cycle; `stats.stale` and `open_runs` of `GET /api/live-status` count them.

- **A free change detector with the events of the account** (`events_feed.enabled`, off by default; `events_feed.interval_seconds`, `events_feed.owner_kind`, `events_feed.max_per_tick`): the monitor reads the events of the organization or the user with an ETag. A read that finds nothing new answers `304` and costs no allowance; when a push, a pull request, a tag or a release shows up, only the repository named in the event is read again in the next cycle, instead of waiting for the slow turn of the cold repositories. GitHub's `X-Poll-Interval` wins when it is longer. No webhook and no tunnel are needed; the counters are `feed` in `GET /api/live-status`.

- **The state of GitHub in the bar, and the reds that appear during an incident** (`github_status.enabled`, off by default; `github_status.interval_seconds`, `github_status.mark_reds`): the monitor reads the public summary of githubstatus.com (one small JSON, no account, nothing about you is sent), shows the state of GitHub in the bar at the bottom of the page and tells once when an incident of Actions starts and ends (the notification kinds `github_incident` and `github_incident_over`). While the incident lasts, a red that appears says "possible cause: an incident of GitHub" in its text and in the field `possible_cause`; the red itself is never hidden.
- **Hold the notices of an incident of GitHub** (`github_status.quiet_notices`, off by default): while GitHub Actions has an incident, the notices of the reds marked "possible cause: an incident of GitHub" wait and are told once, with how many are still red, when the incident is over (they are never held longer than six hours). The reds stay in the feed and on the page.

- **An outside heartbeat, so that somebody notices when the monitor stops** (`heartbeat.enabled`, off by default; `heartbeat.url_env`, `heartbeat.interval_seconds`, `heartbeat.send_state`, `heartbeat.fail_suffix`): every few minutes the monitor pings an address you keep in an environment variable (a secret: never written in the configuration, shown or logged) and a service such as healthchecks.io, Cronitor or Uptime Kuma raises the alarm when the pings stop. The ping carries at most the version and the state; a monitor whose refresh loop is stuck does not send it, so the silence is the alarm (or it is told at once through a failure address). `GET /api/health` shows the counters.

- **An assistant for the credentials: the installation of the GitHub App is found by itself and a button tests the token** (`github_app.installation_id` can stay empty; `POST /api/credential/probe`, this computer only; the button "Test the token" of the Quota tab): the monitor now finds the installation of the account it watches, or the only one the App has, instead of taking the first one it is shown; with several accounts and none of them the one of the monitor it lists them so one can be written. The test says who the login is, whether it is a classic token (and which scopes it lacks) or a fine-grained token or an App, how many repositories it sees (and whether single sign-on cuts the list), how much allowance is left and, for the App, its name, its installation and the permissions it lacks. At most one real check is made every 10 seconds; nothing is written to GitHub and no token or key is shown.

- **The webhook without a public address, through the WebSocket of GitHub** (`webhook.transport` = `websocket`; `webhook.ws_targets`, `webhook.ws_max_targets`, `webhook.ws_cleanup`; off by default): the monitor runs and watches `gh webhook forward` for each repository or organization you list, which makes a temporary webhook and hands every delivery to the receiver of the monitor from a connection opened by this computer, so nothing is exposed and no tunnel is needed. It writes to GitHub (the temporary hooks) and needs a token that can manage webhooks; a forwarder that ends is started again with a longer wait, and one whose token may not manage hooks says `forbidden` in `forwarders` of `GET /api/live-status`. The deliveries are signed with a secret made at every start that only the receiver of this process accepts next to the shared one.

- **Recovery of the webhook deliveries a switched-off computer missed** (`webhook.redeliver.enabled`, off by default; `webhook.redeliver.hooks`, `webhook.redeliver.max_per_run`, `webhook.redeliver.max_age_hours`, `webhook.redeliver.interval_minutes`; `POST /api/webhook/redeliver`): a minute after the start and then every hour, the monitor reads the recent deliveries of the webhooks you name and asks GitHub to send again the ones that no attempt answered with a 2xx, with a limit per look, a growing wait (5 to 300 seconds) and a stop after the eighth ask. What was asked is remembered in a private file, so a restart does not ask again at once; the operator can ask now, and the request leaves a line in the log of actions. It writes to GitHub, so it is off and only touches the hooks you name; `redelivery` of `GET /api/live-status` has the counters.
- **Histograms of the jobs, billing and runners in `/metrics`** (`outputs.prometheus.histograms`, `outputs.prometheus.per_runner` and `outputs.prometheus.histogram_max_series`, off by default): built in memory from the signed `workflow_job` and `workflow_run` events, `ghamon_job_wait_seconds` (time in the queue by runner group and label, counted once per job), `ghamon_job_run_seconds`, `ghamon_workflow_run_seconds`, `ghamon_jobs_total` and the demand for runners now (`ghamon_runner_jobs_waiting`, `ghamon_runner_oldest_wait_seconds`), with a limit of series per histogram (the rest is counted as `other`). The billing report of the month (`quota.billing_api`) is written as `ghamon_billing_*` (total, included, paid, by SKU, in dollars), the self-hosted runners as `ghamon_runners{state}` and, per runner, their state, jobs, failed jobs and busy seconds (also in `GET /api/runners/model` as `per_runner`). A scrape never reads GitHub.

- **The cycle and its circuit in `/metrics`**: duration of the last refresh cycle and of its phases and the histogram of all of them (`ghamon_tick_*`), cycles and failed cycles, when the last one ended well, reads repeated after a passing failure (`ghamon_read_retries_total`) and the repositories that wait before the next read because they failed several times in a row (`ghamon_repos_circuit_open`, `ghamon_repos_read_failing`).

- **Queue of reads with backpressure** (`backpressure.enabled`, `backpressure.max_reads_per_cycle`, `backpressure.shed_percent` and `backpressure.min_reads`, off by default): the reads of a cycle go through one bounded queue (red repositories first, then hot ones, never read ones and the oldest read), what does not fit waits for the next cycle, and a cycle longer than the set share of the pause cuts the allowed reads in half (they grow back by a tenth after each fast cycle). Visible as `ghamon_read_queue_*`.

- **The allowance by account and bucket, and the tightest bucket in the header** (`quota.chip`, on by default): `GET /api/quota` carries `accounts`, the buckets (core, graphql, search...) of every source with the share used and the renewal time, and the header shows the bucket with the least left when it is not the core one, how many others are in use and the breakdown on hover. Nothing new is read: it uses the free `rate_limit` call the cycle already makes.

- **Observability pack** in `docs/grafana/`: `ghamon_alert_rules.yml` (rules for Prometheus with their PromQL: a monitor that does not answer, red workflows, allowance and billing, slow or stalled cycles, repositories in the circuit, waits for a runner by histogram, runners offline), `alertmanager.example.yml` (routes to a webhook, Grafana OnCall and n8n; every address is an example) and `n8n_ghamon_alerts.json` (a flow that receives the alerts). Explained in `docs/OUTPUTS.md` in the eight languages.

- **Limits and capacity** (`limits.warn_percent` and `limits.memory_mb`): the doctor has a new check `capacity` that compares the setup with its ceilings (calls an hour against the allowance and the budget of the sampler, memory of the held runs, repositories against `discover_limit`) and warns before they are reached; `/metrics` shows the live ones as `ghamon_limit_used_percent` (events kept, streams, read queue, series of the histograms).

- **One read for everybody** (`refresh.share_by_workflow` and `refresh.share_seconds`, off by default): when the cycle, a page, a wall screen or an API client ask for the jobs of the same run at the same moment, the first one reads from GitHub and the others take its answer; a failed read is never remembered. `/metrics` counts the reads spared.

- **A notification is not repeated** (`notify.dedupe_ledger`, `notify.dedupe_hours` and `notify.max_event_age_hours`, off by default): a small ledger in the data folder (hashes and times, no text) stops the same notification after a page reload, after a restart and when a pipeline leaves the window for a while; an event whose own date is older than the set hours is not notified. The guide says that the Microsoft Teams channel takes the address of a Power Automate Workflows flow.

- **Cost of the CI by kind of change and a carbon estimate** (`finops.by_change`, `finops.co2`, `finops.co2_watts` and `finops.co2_grams_per_kwh`, off by default): the runs of the last 24 hours by dependencies, scheduled, manual, release, feature, fix, docs, ci, test, refactor, perf, chore or other, with minutes, share and the estimated dollars of the private repositories (`by_change` in `GET /api/quota` and `ghamon_ci_change_*` in `/metrics`); the carbon is an order of magnitude from your own watts and grams per kWh, never a report of emissions. Local reusable workflows (files that only other workflows of the repository call) are left out of these figures.

- **OpenTelemetry with the conventions of CI/CD and one span per job** (`otlp.cicd_conventions` and `otlp.job_spans`, off by default): the span of a run can carry the attributes of the OpenTelemetry semantic conventions for CI/CD (pipeline name, run id and link, result, attempt, repository and branch) and be named `RUN <workflow>`; each finished job can be sent as a child span of its run (runner, group, labels, time in the queue, result), from the signed `workflow_job` events of the webhook.

- **Several workspaces** (`workspaces.list` and `workspaces.active`, off while the list is empty): the folders of local clones are named as `name=folder` and the active one is chosen by name; `MONITOR_ROOT` still wins and `workspaces.active` wins over `workspace_root`.

- **Long windows of the comparison on a big account** (`compare.progressive` and `compare.progressive_days`, off by default): a window longer than the days set is answered at once with its last days, marked `partial`, while the whole one is computed in the background and given to the next request.

- **`monitor_cli.py toolbox`**: the small tools of the operator that work on this computer with no monitor running: `limits` (the setup against its ceilings), `workspaces`, `ledger` (the notifications already sent, `--force` forgets them), `summary` (the events by kind, repository and day) and `export-events FILE` (jsonl, csv, parquet or duckdb by the end of the name; parquet and duckdb need their package, the file must be new, nothing is overwritten and no query language is accepted).
- **A library of more than sixty sounds** (`sound.voices`, `screens.sound_voices`, `screens.sound_volume`): every kind of event keeps the sound it always had and can be given any other sound of the library, for the page, for the beep of this computer and, with its own list and volume, for the wall screens. The sounds are made in the browser (nothing to download), none lasts more than a second and every one is different; one source (`soundlib.py`) is copied into the page and the wall screens by `tools/make_sound_library.py`, and a test checks that the copies are current. The eight guides of the sounds name every one.

- **An optional background ambience and a clock tick** (`sound.ambient`, `sound.ambient_volume`, `sound.tick`, `sound.tick_seconds`; both off by default): the ambience is two very soft low notes that turn rougher while something is red, and the tick is a very short dry click every few seconds so a frozen screen is heard. Both follow the mute chip, the main switch and the quiet hours, in the page and in the wall screens.
- **The command line writes a clean output without a terminal** (`--color auto|always|never`, `NO_COLOR`): with the output redirected, piped to `tee` or run by cron it writes no escape sequence, takes the width from `COLUMNS` or 80, cleans the escape sequences that a title or a name from GitHub may carry, and a reader that closes early (`| head`) ends the command quietly instead of with a traceback. Only the header of `--format table` is ever coloured.

- **`monitor_cli.py metrics` writes the figures for cron and for mail**: one `name=value` line per figure, in a fixed order and nothing else on the standard output, and with `--if-problem` nothing at all (exit 0) when there is no red workflow and no alert at `--fail-on` or worse, so cron sends mail only the day something is wrong (exit 5 with the figures).

- **The notice of a newer version names the command of your way of installing**: the unpacked product, a service manager, a container, pipx, uv or an extension of `gh` (`channel` and `command` in the answer of `/api/about`, and in the console and the About panel). Nothing is run by the notice.
- **Guards of the service helper of the updater**: with `updates.systemd_helper` (or `--systemd-unit`) it refuses to stop or start a service that belongs to another installation, refuses to start it while something else answers on its port, does not take a restart for done while the process is still the old one, and `update status --systemd-unit UNIT` lists only the errors since the last start of the service.

- **The release of GitHub waits for the package**: the image workflow now starts on the version tag, publishes the image to the registry and publishes the release (created as a draft) only when the image answers by its tag for both platforms.

- **The CI runs the suite with the network cut and audits its dependencies**: a job runs every test in a network namespace that has only the loopback, and a job runs `pip-audit` through `tools/audit_dependencies.py`, where a suppression carries a reason and an expiry date and an expired or dead suppression fails the job.

- **A weekly smoke test of the external sources**: the workflow `sources-smoke.yml` reads the real status page of GitHub, the advisories, the description of the REST API and the last release of the project, and fails when a source changed a format that the monitor reads (`tools/smoke_sources.py`; a test keeps its list of endpoints equal to the ones the code calls).

- **The workflow linter finds the trap of the inputs with a hyphen in a container action** (rule `container_input_name`, off by default, `warn`): GitHub hands `fail-on-findings` to the program as `INPUT_FAIL-ON-FINDINGS`, so a script that reads `INPUT_FAIL_ON_FINDINGS` never sees the value; the finding names the script, the line and the input.

- **A storage that stops answering gives a clean 503**: the routes that need the database answer `503` with `Retry-After` and `storage_unavailable` instead of dropping the connection, the monitor keeps running and the next request after the storage is back is served; the live stream tells the browser how long to wait before it reconnects (`retry:`) and resumes with `Last-Event-ID`. An end-to-end test cuts the database of a real server to check it.

- **The monitor watches its own memory and threads** (`selfwatch.enabled`, `selfwatch.rss_warn_mb` 1024, `selfwatch.threads_warn` 400, `selfwatch.interval_seconds` 60): `/api/health` carries `selfwatch`, going over a limit is noted once in the counter of swallowed failures with the numbers and releases what it can, and the CI runs the monitor with an account of 120 workflows in a container of one processor and 512 MB (`tools/limits_check.py`).

- **Actions on GitHub need a reason and an operator, and the server guards them** (`github_actions.require_reason`, `github_actions.operator`, `github_actions.comment_on_target`, `github_actions.preflight`, `github_actions.writer_lock`): a plan without a reason (3 to 200 characters) and an operator is refused by the server whatever the page sends, both are written in the log, the target is looked at again right before acting (`stale` if it changed), the credential is asked without side effects whether it can (a doctor of permissions that also reads the list of who may push), only one monitor of the computer writes and a monitor started with `--profile qa` is an observer. With `github_actions.comment_on_target` on, a closed issue or pull request also gets a comment with them.
- **The progress stream of an update ends with its session too**: `GET /api/updates/stream` now looks at the session every `auth.stream_revalidate_min` minutes like the other two streams, so no live stream keeps permissions that are gone. A test fails if a stream of events is added without the guard, or a server of WebSockets without the same revalidation.
- **The profile `qa` for a test monitor** (`--profile qa` or `MONITOR_PROFILE=qa`): the monitor starts as an observer that refuses every action on GitHub, listens on another port (the usual one plus 100, unless `--port` is given; the command line follows the same rule when the variable is set) and keeps its ETag cache in a temporary file that is deleted when it ends, so a test instance can never act twice on the same account or mix its cache with the real one.

- **The live streams end when their session ends** (`auth.stream_revalidate_min`, 10): a live stream looks at the session that opened it again every few minutes and closes when it was revoked or has expired, the user was removed or the role no longer allows it, so no connection keeps permissions that are gone.

- **The monitor asks the search engines to stay out and says what a network exposure means** (`listen.noindex`, on): every answer carries `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` and `/robots.txt` disallows everything; the exposure check of the doctor tells the address, whether there is TLS and whether people sign in, and reminds that a page open to anyone may be marked as phishing. Tests with the headers of Caddy and of a Cloudflare Tunnel keep the check of Host and Origin behind a proxy honest.

- **A table of the network destinations of the monitor and what fails if they are blocked** (`monitor_cli.py doctor --egress`): every host the current configuration can call, what for, the setting that makes it a destination and the consequence of blocking it, for a computer behind a strict outbound policy; a test keeps the table equal to the hosts the code names.

- **Events towards a SIEM** (optional, off; `siem.sinks`, `siem.queue_max`): Elasticsearch (bulk), Splunk HEC, Loki, Microsoft Sentinel (signed with the shared key), BigQuery or a plain HTTP receiver, each with its own filters (`from_minutes`, `levels`, `repos_filter`), a closed list of fields, the token read from an environment variable and never from the file, the network rules of the monitor (no private address unless the sink allows it), a bounded queue when a receiver is down and nothing at all in the offline mode.

- **The MCP server has guided prompts, describes itself, links to the page and can write under control** (`mcp.prompts` on, `mcp.write_tools` off): the prompts `am_i_affected`, `investigate_red` and `weekly_review` give the AI a numbered plan; the tools `describe_capabilities`, `ping` and `whoami` say what the server can do; the answers about alerts, diagnoses and the allowance carry `dashboard_url`; and the tool `gh_action`, which exists only when the setting is on, plans an action, shows a preview and sends nothing until the same call comes back with `confirm: true` and the plan, through the same guards as the page.

- **The workflow linter of the CI sentinel reads the default branch of the remote, not the working tree of the clone**: the workflow files of a local clone are taken with `git show origin/<default branch>` (no fetch, no network), like the CHANGELOG and the versions, so a clone left on an old branch no longer makes the linter judge a workflow that main does not have; without a remote branch it reads the working tree and says so.

- **A release is suggested only from five entries under [Unreleased]**: `sentinel.candidate_min_entries` now starts at 5 (it was 1), so the release candidates of the CI sentinel and the release readiness no longer appear for one or two small changes. A value you chose yourself is kept; the number can be set from 1 to 100 in Settings.

- **Releases are published as immutable releases**: the order of a release is a DRAFT first, then every file (`github_actions_monitor.zip`, `SHA256SUMS`, `monitor_updater.py`) is uploaded and checked, and only then the release is published or, in the image-first order, the tag is pushed so that `release-image.yml` publishes it; once public a release accepts no new file. `CONTRIBUTING` (eight languages) describes the order. Turning on the setting of GitHub is the maintainer's choice and nothing here turns it on.
- **A CI job analyses the workflows with zizmor**: `.github/workflows/zizmor.yml` calls the shared reusable workflow, pinned by its full commit SHA, on every pull request, every push to main and by hand; only findings of high severity fail it, the rest are listed in the summary, and the reviewed ones are ignored with their reason in `.github/zizmor.yml`. It has no permission beyond reading and uses no secret.
- **Time saved, as an estimate and in the analytics** (`productivity.enabled`, off by default): the Analytics section can show how many minutes a week the monitor saves, task by task (watching the workflows and finding the cause of a failed run), with the formula of the productivity guide. The minutes without the monitor are notes the person writes in the settings (`productivity.*`, nine settings with their help): a task without them says `no_data` and no number is made up; the failed runs are counted by the monitor from the runs it has in view; a saving that comes from the assumed percentage is labelled `assumed`, and one from the person's own minutes with the monitor `measured`. Fixing a failure is not counted. It is in `/api/analytics` (`time_saved`), in the summary of the A12 panel, in the demo with data and in the guides and skills; it makes no call to GitHub.
- **`monitor_cli alerts --since 24h` and a history of what was open**: the monitor now keeps, in the file of the silences, when each alert was first seen, last seen and closed (`GET /api/alerts?since=24h` adds `since`); an alert counts as closed only after ten minutes of absence and never when a sentinel failed to answer, so a restart or a broken read closes nothing. The command lists the alerts that were open at some moment of that time, closed ones too, and says so when the monitor is too old to keep the history. The history is local, holds at most 500 alerts for 90 days and reads nothing from GitHub.

- **`monitor_cli proposals` and approval before an action of the AI runs** (`ai.actions.require_approval`, off by default): `proposals list|show ID|approve ID|reject ID|execute ID` works on the actions the AI proposed, from this computer only. `approve` and `reject` record who decided (`POST /api/ai/actions/review`, in the audit log, nothing runs); `execute` goes through the same door as the page (the exact command, `ai.actions.execute`, the hourly limit, the local log). With the setting on, a proposal runs only after it was approved; a rejected one never runs, whatever the setting. `GET /api/ai/actions` lists the waiting proposals without their arguments.

- **Two more checks in the doctor, `compare_window` and `etag`**: the first says whether the comparison of two periods can look back two windows with the history it asks for (`compare.min_days_of_history`) and keeps (`retention.events_days`), with the numbers; the second gives the share of conditional reads that GitHub answered with 304 in the monitor that runs here, and warns when answers come back without an ETag, because then nothing is spared. `/api/quota` gives the counters of the ETag cache in its list of ways to read less. Read only; both are fine when the feature is off or no monitor runs.
- **The combined state of a commit also counts the open pull requests** (`sentinel.surface.checks.commit_state.include_pr_heads`, off by default, and `pr_heads_max`, 5): with it on, the head commit of each open pull request (the newest ones, drafts left out) is looked at with its checks from every app, and a row `repository#number` shows the red or pending ones; a red pull request is a row, not a failure of the branch, and a head or a list that cannot be read is listed as not evaluable, never as green. It costs one call for the list and one for each pull request; it is in the guide of the sentinel and in the settings help.
- **The updater shows the whole log of its process and can discard a copy**: the Updates menu of the page reads the last lines of the log of the process of the last update (`GET /api/updates/log`, values after token, secret or password hidden) and lists the copies of the folder of copies (`GET /api/updates/copies`) with a **Discard copy** button for each one (`POST /api/updates/discard`, administrator, this computer only, recorded). It asks first, twice for the copy the way back needs, and never for the copy of an update that is running; only that copy is deleted, never the installation or the data. The updater has the same as commands: `copies`, `discard --name COPY [--force] [--yes]` and `log [--lines N]`.
- **Fresh, stale and expired answers for the heavy queries, with a time ceiling** (`cache.heavy_fresh_s` 5, `cache.stale_serve_h` 1, `analytics.query_timeout_s` 120): the answers of the comparison and of the optional reports are kept in memory; a fresh one is served as it is, one a little old is served at once marked `X-Monitor-Freshness: stale` (with its age) while a single computation refreshes it in the background, and one older than that, or never made, is computed now (a copy that old is never served). A query that takes longer than its ceiling is answered `504` with the code `query_timeout` instead of making the request wait for ever, and a slow query is never started twice for the same question. No call to GitHub is added; it is in the guide of the comparison and in the settings help.

### Fixed

- **The signing queue of A5 no longer offers what does not have to be signed**: a run enters it only when it is not a dry run (the step "Prepare the draft" ran), a draft release is waiting or the repository has no published release, and the version is not already published with its signature. The five false rows (four manual builds whose version was already published and signed, and one dry run whose `--sign --publish --bench` command would have been wrong) are gone. Each run of the queue is looked at once every six hours (the jobs of the run and the releases of its repository, two reads, at most three runs a cycle) and what leaves is listed under the queue as "checked: already published vX" or "dry run" instead of "not checked".

- **The Security sentinel no longer says that a repository has no branch protection when a ruleset protects it** (`security.branch_unprotected`): a repository protected only by a ruleset answers 404 to the classic endpoint, so 135 repositories were flagged by mistake. The branch counts as protected with the classic protection or an active rule on it (the `protected` flag of the branch and `rules/branches/<branch>` are read when the classic one is missing). `signals.branch.require` can name rule types that the rules must have (`pull_request`, `required_status_checks`...), and a protected branch that lacks one is flagged with the missing ones; the default is an empty list.

- **A repository that the Security sentinel could not read says why**: the errors of the status carry the message and a cause (`timeout`, `rate`, `network`, `error`) and `error_kinds` counts them; a failed read is a gap of coverage, never a finding. The reads of the Delivery and Supply chain sentinels that time out are listed with the reason `timeout` (in the eight languages) instead of a plain `error`.

- **`release.ecosystem_drift` is information (`info`) by default**: the pins of the consumers of a shared contract are brought up by the release train of the ecosystem, not by hand, so a pin that is behind no longer counts as a warning. The pin table is read from the default branch of the provider and compared with its latest published release, as before; `signals.ecosystem_drift.level` raises it again.

- **The AI hears the Security, Delivery and Supply chain sentinels**: their alerts are now the signals `sentinel.security`, `sentinel.delivery` and `sentinel.supply` of `GET /api/ai/state` (one row per code and repository, worst first) and, when they are `watch` or `act`, one event per code and repository in `ai_alerts.jsonl`, the stream, the hook and `ALERTS_BRIEF.md` (at most 25 per sentinel and one more event that counts the rest; `info` alerts stay in the state). Every sentinel, Collab included, adds `sentinel.<name>.coverage` when it could not read everything (failed reads with their cause, repositories not checked yet, a pause, sources it may not read): an empty list of alerts is not a clean one while it is open.

- **The Releases panel (A5), the readiness table (A11) and the release candidates tool count the entries of the CHANGELOG of main, not of the branch the clone is on**: the count was read from the working tree of the clone, so a clone left on an old branch of an agent showed 20 entries where main has 3 (and 13 where main has 2), and the version it compared with the last release was the one of that branch. They now read `CHANGELOG.md`, the manifest and `pyproject` from the default branch of the remote that the clone knows (`git show origin/<default>:<file>`, never a fetch, nothing in the clone is changed); when that branch is not known they read the working tree and the row says it ("clone on branch X, not on main; counted from the working tree"), and when the clone was not fetched since GitHub moved main it says that too. ONE function counts for the whole monitor (`delivery/mainsource.py`): every list line under `## [Unreleased]` counts as one entry, nested ones included, with CRLF or LF, as before. The title of the candidates table says that the count is the one of main.
- **A6 no longer says that the `dependencies` label is missing when the repository has it**: the search of labels of GitHub answered nothing for a label that exists (a repository with the label was reported), so the monitor now also asks for the label by its exact name.
- **A7 says when a figure is a minimum**: the minutes of a repository come from its latest runs, so a repository with more runs than the sample (151 runs in a day against the 20 it keeps) showed a fifth of its real use as if it were the whole. The figure of a repository whose sample is full and does not reach back to the start of the window is now marked with ≥ in the table, the summary and the text of the panel.
- **A9 does not claim that the seeding never ran when its runs could not be read**: when the runs of the workflow could not be read (quota, network) every board was reported as "never ran"; the tab now shows the error only. The definitions and their date are read from the default branch of the remote, so a clone that is behind or on another branch cannot make a board look as if it had drifted.
- **The tag against manifest check (A10) reads the manifest and the CHANGELOG of main**: a clone behind after a release showed "the tag is later than the manifest" until the next scan.

- **The rows that a wall screen hides to fit a list now really disappear**: the page hid a row with the hidden attribute, but a row that is itself a flex box ignored it, so the list of events, the queue, the failures and the rows of the heat map were cut in half at the bottom of their card, over the footer. The hiding now wins over any style.
- **Headings, labels and figures of the wall screens no longer end in dots or leave their box, in any of the eight languages**: a heading takes a second line, a figure wider than its box (seven digits, a long unit) gets a smaller type down to a floor, the tag next to the title and the label of a legend, a severity or a tile wrap or shorten instead of pushing the clock out, the heat map keeps a height for each row, and no type of the wall screens is smaller than 0.8 of the base size.
- **In the kiosk the column chooser of the tables is no longer shown**: nobody can click it on a wall, and when its box was open it covered the table of repositories.

- **The Teams channel always sends its `Content-Length` and uses an Adaptive Card for the Workflows of Teams**: a Teams endpoint that refuses a body sent in pieces no longer fails, and the address of a Workflows flow (`logic.azure.com`, `powerplatform.com`) gets the message as a card, which that kind of flow reads; the older connector of a channel still gets plain text. A test with a fake server that refuses bodies sent in pieces checks it.

### Changed

- **The score, the reports, the mirror and the analytics now read every run by default** (`score.source` is `ledger`, it was `recent600`): an account that reads thousands of runs in a cycle no longer gets a figure made from the newest 600 only. The migration record carries the change: whoever never set `score.source` gets the new default, whoever set it by hand keeps the value (`recent600` included, with the updater's options to pin the old default or adopt the new one). Set `score.source` to `recent600` to go back. No call to GitHub is added; it is in the guide of the score, the settings help and the example file.
- **One bad element never stops a round of a sentinel, and what was not read is held and named, in every sentinel**: each repository, file, item and stage of the loops of the CI, Collab, Security, Supply and Delivery sentinels is read inside its own protection (a pull request or an issue that cannot be read, a workflow file the linter cannot process, a stage of the CI tick that fails no longer take the repository or the stages after them with them), the failure is written down with its cause and the errors of a repository that left the scope leave with it. A repository whose alerts or settings were never read is listed by name as not checked (`unchecked` in the status, the line "Not checked yet" of `read_report.py`) instead of counting as zero alerts. The reading passes of the CI sentinel have a time ceiling, `sentinel.deadline_s` (30 seconds, 0 = none), the list of runners names the repositories it did not read (`unchecked` in `/api/runners`), and a test of contract fails for any sentinel of the registry that does not keep these rules. No call to GitHub is added.
- **A target that could not be read is never a clean bill of health or a defect, outside the score too**: the health of an ecosystem (readiness) no longer takes a failed read of the alerts of a repository for "no alerts" (that repository is listed under `unread`, lowers the coverage and, below the minimum, leaves the ecosystem without a number instead of 100), and the list of release candidates tells a repository without a release from a release it could not read: the second one is named as not evaluated with its cause, is not listed as a candidate and the command ends with an error. No call to GitHub is added.

## [2.1.5]

- **The score, the reports, the mirror and the analytics can read every run, not only the newest 600** (`score.source`, `recent600` by default, `ledger` on request): on an account that reads thousands of runs in a cycle the page keeps the newest 600, and the score was made from them. With `ledger` they are made from every run the cycle read, counted once in the ledger, which now also keeps hourly totals (successes and failures per hour) that survive its ceiling of 20000 runs; a window the ceiling could not hold whole says so (`data_quality.truncated`) and gives the totals the hours hold (`data_quality.aggregate`). With the default the snapshot says when the cycle read more than the score saw (`data_quality.cut`, `data_quality.runs_read`, the reason `recent_cut` and a line on the page). The default becomes `ledger` in the next version. A ledger and the snapshots of earlier versions are read as they are. No call to GitHub is added; it is in the guide of the score, the settings help, the guides and the skills.
- **Every command of the command line takes `--format text|table|plain|json|ndjson`**: `table` fits the columns to the width of the terminal and counts wide Japanese and Chinese characters as two cells, `plain` is one tab-separated line per record for `grep` and `awk`, and `ndjson` is one JSON object per line. `text` is what every command wrote before and stays the default (`cli.default_format` changes it); `--json` is unchanged.
- **`watch status|alerts` repeats the read of the command** (`--interval`, `--once`, `--no-clear`): it reads the monitor on this computer, never GitHub, keeps a floor under the interval (`cli.watch.min_interval_seconds`, 2 by default), retries a monitor that cannot be reached with a longer pause each time and ends with exit code 0 on Ctrl-C.
- **`watch --exit-on-change` ends with the new exit code 6 when the set of alerts or the figures of the status change**: a clock that ticks is not a change, and without the flag the code 6 never appears. The codes 0 to 5 are unchanged and a test keeps the table of the guide and the code in agreement.
- **`completions bash|zsh|fish|powershell` prints the script that completes the commands, words and options**, written from the real parser so it cannot fall behind (a test compares both). It only prints: it never edits a file of yours.
- **A kiosk profile for a TV or a wall, off by default** (`kiosk.enabled`): `monitor_cli.py kiosk ops|team|release|quiet` prints the address of a preset, and the page, only when the setting is on and the address has `kiosk=1`, makes the type larger (`kiosk.font_scale_percent`, 150 by default), can use a high contrast (`kiosk.high_contrast`) and can turn through the tabs of the preset (`kiosk.rotate_views`, `kiosk.rotate_seconds`).
- **Two new guides in eight languages, `CLI_OPERATOR.md` and `KIOSK.md`**: the command line for the operator (formats, `watch`, `completions`, exit codes and `jq` examples that a test runs) and the kiosk; the guide of the command line and the one of the screens point to them.

- **The wall screens take a repository filter (`?repo=WORD,WORD`)**: `/screen/overview` and `/screen/now` keep only the repositories whose name holds one of the words, as the page, the kiosk and the tab screens already did. A repository whose name the screen hides is never matched, so the filter cannot be used to find out what a hidden name contains; the figures (fleet, red, queue, events, alerts) follow the rows that are kept, the score and the map of groups of the whole fleet are not shown while a filter is on, and `/screen/sentinels` is not filtered. The Screens menu offers the parameter for every wall screen and the window names the filter next to its number. Guide `SCREENS.md` in the eight languages.

### Changed

- **Each AI icon of the bar opens its own group of the Settings**: the chip (the AI built into the monitor) now scrolls to the provider and model group of the AI tab and the waves (external AI tools) to the group of the feed they read, instead of both landing on the same place. The group is brought into view, flashed for a moment and its first control gets the focus; a search typed in the Settings is cleared first so the target is never hidden. The tooltip of each icon now says where the click leads, in the eight languages.

- **In the Docker Compose file the monitor believes one address, the one of Caddy, and not the whole network**: with the profile `proxy`, Caddy now has a fixed address (`MONITOR_PROXY_IP`, 172.29.77.250 by default, inside `MONITOR_SUBNET`) and `MONITOR_TRUSTED_PROXIES` names that address. Another container of the network that skips Caddy can no longer choose its own address with `X-Forwarded-For`; the Docker check proves it with a container that lies at every try. If you already named the whole network in `MONITOR_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, put the address of Caddy there instead.

- **The example file of the configuration now carries every setting, and the page of Settings shows a default for all of them**: the AI settings (the model, the limits and the provider of each of the five tasks, the fallback chain, the budgets, the private addresses, the record of prompts, the hidden words and patterns), the read pool (which source answers each kind of read first and how many calls each keeps free), the three folders, the days after which a silence expires, the application id of the GitHub App and the private addresses of the notifications. Each value is what the code does when the setting is absent, a test compares them, and the five per-task time limits that said 30 seconds now say 0, which is what they are: the task uses the general limit. The three settings that are computed (the state of the signing of the webhook and of Slack and Discord) are shown but never written.

- **The walk through the page also tells a monitor that fails**: besides script and console errors, an answer with a 5xx status to a call of the page is now a problem of the walk (with its address, without the query), and the walk can be made at the size of a desktop and of a phone (`--size desktop mobile`).

### Added

- **Every run that is going is counted, workflow by workflow** (`fleet.show_all_active`, on by default): the health of a repository has `active` (shown as *Going now* in the repository dialog) with how many runs of each workflow run and wait and the newest run of each branch, so two runs of the same workflow that go at once (releases in parallel, one run per branch) are not shown as one; the rows of the fleet carry `running` and `queued`. Off: a workflow shows only its newest run.

- **What the plan of the account does not have is told apart from a refusal, and it is not counted against the sentinel** (`sentinels.security.plan_na`, on by default): when GitHub answers that a check needs another plan (branch protection or code scanning of a private repository on the free plan) the Security sentinel shows it as `N/A (plan)`, a refusal as `N/A (no access)` and a feature that is off as `N/A (off)` (`unavailable_why` in the status, a line under the sub-panels with a tooltip). The pairs of the first kind are left out of the readable count and of the coverage of the score and counted apart (`sources_na_plan`); none of the three is a finding and none is a clean bill of health, so a plan with none of what weighs gives `unknown` and no security part of the score. Off: they count as sources that could not be read, as before.
- **Actions of another private organisation that the login cannot see can be left out of the supply chain findings** (`sentinels.supply.ignore_uses`, empty by default): names `owner/repo` (with `*` for any) whose unpinned or moving references are not reported on a guess; they are not hidden either, because the status counts them apart (`uses_left_out`).
- **When an allowance runs out, the read is made with another source and, if all are spent, the last good copy is shown with its age** (`quota.failover`, off by default, with `quota.failover_max_age_minutes`): a read that answers 429 or "rate limit exceeded" is made once more with another source that can read (your login, the GitHub App, the second account) and the one that ran dry counts as spent until its next free check; when every source is spent the last good copy of that read is shown with its age instead of an error, never older than the limit asked for. A limit is never taken for a refusal of permission, a write never fails over, and the status of the quota says how many times it acted.
- **The daily trend can mark the days a workflow file changed** (`sentinel.trend_workflow_marks`, off by default, with `sentinel.trend_workflow_marks_per_tick`, `sentinel.trend_workflow_marks_hours` and `sentinel.trend_workflow_marks_days`): a square on the chart on the days a commit touched a file of `.github/workflows` in a repository, next to the marks of the releases, so a jump in duration or in failures has its explanation beside it. It costs one request per repository, a few repositories a round and each again only after the hours asked; only the days the daily history already has are marked, and the marks are in `a3.trend.workflows` of `GET /api/sentinel`.
- **The workflow linter can read a public repository with no credential** (`lint.anonymous_public`, off by default; `lint_workflows.py --remote OWNER/REPO --anon`): to look at the workflows of a third party the files come from `raw.githubusercontent.com`, which spends none of the allowance of your token, and the listing of the folder is an anonymous request; a file that did not change is not read again, a file over 256 KiB is skipped, no header with a secret is sent, an air-gapped monitor never reads and a private or missing repository says "not found" and never "clean".

- **Every tab says what the wall screens do with it**: the decision of the wall screens is now written tab by tab (`TAB_DECISIONS` in `screens.py`): Now is on Right now; Alerts, Charts and Fleet are on the overview; every sentinel tab is on Sentinels; and the tabs that are not shown say why (`people`, `controls`, `reading` or `text`). The guide of the screens lists the table in the eight languages, and a test fails when a tab is added without its decision or a guide lacks a row.
- **The demo with data now fills every tab that the plain demo leaves empty**: `tools/demo_monitor.py --rich` adds, besides the findings, the daily trend, the comparison, the book of failures and one scheduled report it already had, a saved report of the day before (so Yesterday vs today shows what appeared and what disappeared) and the four optional reports switched on with invented findings (workflow hygiene, queue by runner, release ready or blocked, supply chain). Every tab is listed in `TAB_DEMO` as `plain` or `rich` with what it gets, and a test starts the demo and reads each rich tab through its own endpoint.

- **An optional look at the whole state of the last commit, as GitHub shows it on a pull request**: `sentinel.surface.checks.commit_state` (off by default) reads the check runs of the last commit of the default branch and says "N of M passed" with the names that failed or are pending. A check is identified by its name and its app (two Apps can publish a check with the same name), only the most recent run of each counts (the ones a re-run replaced do not), and `include_other_apps` (on) decides whether the checks of other GitHub Apps count with the ones of Actions, which explains a repository that is green in Actions and red in its pull request. `include_statuses` (off) adds the commit statuses that older services publish, one per context, at the price of one more call per repository. Section of the surface tab, help, label, eight languages, guide, example file, migration, skills and tests included.

- **Runs deleted after the monitor saw them are noticed** (`sentinels.security.signals.runs_deleted.enabled`, off by default): after a leak of secrets, deleting the run that printed them is the usual way to remove the trace, and GitHub leaves no visible record. The Security sentinel keeps a short private list of the runs that matter it has seen (a push to the default branch, a manual run, a `pull_request_target` run) and, when one leaves the list of runs, asks for that single run: an explicit 404 raises `security.runs_deleted` naming the workflow, who started it and the branch; a refusal, a network error or a run older than `check_window_days` (14) never counts, because the retention of the account may have purged it. At most `max_checks` (50) single runs per repository and round.
- **The Supply chain sentinel can lint only what changed** (`sentinels.supply.incremental`, off by default): a repository whose workflow files (their blob SHAs) and rules are the same as in the last good pass is not linted again and its alerts stay exactly as they are, because not looking never closes an alert; a change of any rule, of the ignore list or of the version of the program makes every repository be linted again, and the status shows how many were left unchanged. A choice for repositories you know, not an inherited default.

- **Every alert of the feed says since when it fails and when it was last looked at**: `GET /api/ai/alerts` and the state file of the feed now carry `first_failed` (the first reading that found it red), `last_failed` (the last reading that found it red) and `last_checked` (the last time it was looked at, also after it was closed), so an AI or a person can tell a new regression from old debt and see that a quiet alert was still being watched. An alert kept by an older version gets `first_failed` from the moment it was first seen and no invented dates for the others. The closed list of fields of the hook does not change. Each alert also has a `status` (`failed` while it is open, `suppressed` once a person acknowledged it, `passed` when it is closed), and the brief counts the suppressed ones apart so that an acknowledgement never hides a growing problem. No new setting.

- **A repository can be left out by its visibility, and what each rule left out is counted**: `discovery.hide` now also accepts `public` (with `private`, `fork` and `empty`), and the Basic tab and `GET /api/discovery` show how many repositories each rule left out (`excluded`: archived, empty, private, public, fork), so a repository left out by a filter is counted and never read as clean. A visibility that was not read is never a reason to leave a repository out.

- **A round of the Collab sentinel has a time ceiling too**: `sentinels.collab.deadline_s` (30 seconds by default; 0 = none), like the Security, Supply chain and Delivery sentinels. When it passes, the round starts no new repository, the ones not reached wait for the next round (the one read longest ago goes first) and the status lists them by name as `unchecked` with `round_cut`, instead of counting them as zero alerts.

- **A queued run can be named "no runner has that label"**: with `ci.stuck.no_runner_check` on (off by default; it needs `runners.enabled`), a run that waits for a label that is not GitHub-hosted, not generic and not carried by any runner the monitor lists gets the reason `no_runner` instead of just `queue`, in the run list, the sentinel and the brief. A runner that is not in that list (a larger runner, one of another organisation) would be named wrongly, so it is yours to switch on. It reads the jobs of at most ten queued runs per cycle; nothing is written.

- **A summary for the next report that a person writes, edits and approves, with a draft from the AI when asked**: in Reports a card offers **Draft from the figures** (plain sentences made from the report, no model) and, with `ai.report_summary.enabled` on (off by default) and an AI configured, **Draft with the AI** (the figures go to the AI of the monitor through the same cleaner, budget, cache and chain as every other question). The text is edited in the page and saved with **Save for the next report**: it opens the next report once, in both formats, escaped, with a line that says who wrote it. Nothing is written by itself; the text is cut to 1200 characters and cleaned of markup and secrets. `POST /api/report-files/summary` (administrator, from this computer, audited) takes `draft`, `draft_ai`, `save` or `clear`, and `GET /api/report-files` says whether a summary is waiting. Label, help, tip, guide and the words of the card in the eight languages.

- **An optional helper so that a monitor under a systemd user service can be updated from the page**: with `updates.systemd_helper` on (off by default), a monitor that runs as a systemd USER service (`systemctl --user`) is offered the update; the process that applies it stops that service, applies the update and starts the service again, and when the new version does not come up the previous one is put back and the service started again. It only ever runs `systemctl --user stop`, `start` or `is-active` on a plain unit name, never `sudo`, and a system service, a container, the demo and the Lite edition stay as they were. The command line does the same with `--systemd-unit` for `apply` and `rollback`. The status of the updates says whether the switch is on and which user service the monitor runs under. Label, help, tip and guide in the eight languages.

- **An optional switch to judge the webhook repository by repository**: with `savings.webhook_live_per_repo` on (off by default, and it works together with `savings.webhook_first`), a quiet repository skips its slow read only when an event of ITS OWN arrived in the last `savings.webhook_window_minutes`. A repository whose webhook was never set up, or broke, is read as always while the others are spared; it spares fewer reads. The Webhook panel counts the repositories that are alive by themselves (`live_repos`). Label, help, tip and guide in the eight languages.
