- **Optional look at the runs that are still going** (`sampling.active_probe`, off by default): when the list of runs of a repository is full, two light requests ask for the runs that are `in_progress` and the runs that are `queued`, so a long run that started before the window of the list is not lost or shown as finished.

- **Every record the monitor keeps now carries the version of its schema, and a record from a newer version is left aside and named**: the state of the sentinels, the silences, the feed state and the update notice are written with `schema_version` at the top (the readers never see it; a record from before the mark reads as version 0, as always). A record of a version newer than the program understands is not read half way: that record starts from its defaults and the file is named in the degraded list (`records.newer`), so a downgrade says which file it did not understand.

- **After a restart the page shows the last picture at once, with its age**: after every complete cycle the picture is kept in `last_picture.json` next to the monitor (a private file, replaced whole); when the monitor starts it is shown marked "Data from N min ago" while the first cycle reads GitHub and replaces it. `startup.resume_max_age_hours` (Settings > Basic, 12 by default) is the oldest picture that is shown; 0 keeps and shows nothing and deletes an old file. A file that does not read, of another format or from the future is ignored.

- **A section on what the monitor saves, and a guide to measure it**: the README, in the eight languages, now says, as an estimate and not a measurement, that using the monitor saves about 85-95 % of the time spent watching CI and triaging failures and about 15-25 % of the overall productivity of a team, with a table by task, the assumptions behind it and the cases where the saving is smaller; the guide PRODUCTIVITY gives the method of the calculation and how to measure the saving in your own use.

- **A refusal of GitHub to an action on the page says why**: the answer of `/api/gh-action/confirm` carries `reason` (`forbidden`: the token has no Actions write permission; `not_found`: no such item or a private repository the token cannot see, GitHub answers 404 for both; `method`, `conflict`, `invalid`) and the page prints it in the eight languages next to the refusal instead of a bare "GitHub refused it".
- **The banner of repositories without data groups the same error**: when many repositories fail with the same message, the page shows ONE alert, "N repositories: <error>", and the names stay behind a fold, instead of the first error of a long list.
- **The audit of accesses is rotated and written under the lock of the operating system**, and the tests that read a refusal of the server release its connection: the supervisor and the accounts that write the same file can no longer lose a line while one of them moves it.

- **A round of the Security, Supply chain and Delivery sentinels has a time ceiling**: `sentinels.security.deadline_s`, `sentinels.supply.deadline_s` and `sentinels.delivery.deadline_s` (30 seconds by default; 0 = none). When it passes, the round starts no new repository, the ones not reached keep waiting (the one read longest ago goes first next round) and the status of the sentinel lists them by name as `unchecked` with `round_cut`, instead of counting them as zero alerts.
- **The retention can no longer be shorter than the longest window that reads it**: when the purge runs, `retention.events_days` is raised to two windows of the comparison and a day (while the comparison is on) and `retention.analytics_days` to `analytics.keep_days`. The settings stay as written; the purge plan shows what it really uses and the doctor warns and says what was raised.
- **More switches say what you will see and what they cost**: the tooltip of the sentinels of Security, Supply chain, Delivery and Collaboration, of the runner model, of the assistant of the AI, of similar alerts, of the record of prompts and of the live stream of events now starts with what appears and the cost in requests, in the eight languages, and a test keeps every note tied to a real setting.

- **The alerts of Dependabot, code scanning and secret scanning are read by cursor**: these lists reject `?page=` and hand out the next page in the `Link` header, so the Security sentinel stopped at the first 100. It now follows `rel="next"` up to `pagination.cursor_pages` more pages (3 by default; 0 = only the first), follows only addresses of the API itself and marks the list as cut when more exist.
- **The configuration file is checked when the monitor starts**: a key that nobody reads (a typo, with the closest known key), a value of the wrong type and a number out of its range are named one per line, and the monitor starts with the default of what it could not read. With `config_check.strict` on (off by default) it names them and does not start. What the monitor itself holds as a default is never called a defect.
- **A state or a conclusion GitHub adds tomorrow breaks nothing**: the values the monitor does not know are counted by name (at most twenty for each field), shown as they came and never read as red or as green, and a run that is not an object or has no number is left out while the others are kept. The brief for the AI tools has the signal `schema.drift` when something was seen.
- **Every entry of the conditional-read cache belongs to the credential that read it**: the key now carries a short fingerprint of the token `gh` runs with (never the token), so the same endpoint read with another token has its own entry and a `304` can never hand back the body of another identity. A cache file from before is read as empty once. A test makes four identities read the same endpoint at the same time.
- **A state that is still moving is never answered from the cache**: the jobs of a run, one run, check suites, check runs and the combined status of a commit are always read in full (`cache.etag.skip_stateful`, on), and a list of runs whose last answer still had a run queued or in progress is read in full the next time (`cache.etag.revalidate_active`, on), so a finished run is not hidden behind an old `304`. A test with a transport that keeps saying 304 proves that no `If-None-Match` is sent in those cases.
- **A refusal is remembered for a few minutes, not asked again every cycle**: a repository that answered 404 or 403 (never a rate limit, a timeout or a server error) is not asked again for `cache.negative_ttl_s` seconds (300 by default; 0 = never remembered). The memory is in the process only, belongs to the credential and is forgotten when the repository is invalidated.
- **The health of the conditional reads is visible**: the cache counts the answers that came without an ETag (a proxy that strips it turns the cache into plain reads without a word), the share of calls answered 304, the reads made in full on purpose and the refusals not repeated. They are in `/metrics` (`ghamon_etag_no_etag_total`, `ghamon_etag_skipped_total`, `ghamon_etag_refused_total`) and in the brief for the AI tools (`quota.etag`), which turns to watch when answers without ETag outnumber the 304.
- **A listing of the account that comes back empty is never believed**: when the list of repositories is empty, or keeps fewer than `discovery.min_keep_pct` percent (50 by default; 0 = believe any) of the last good one, the monitor reads it once more and, if it is still suspicious, keeps the last good listing, asks again in five minutes and says so in the brief (`repos.listing`), instead of showing everything green with nothing watched.

- A Screens menu in the top bar lists every kind of screen the monitor has (the page, kiosks, the tab screens, the three wall screens, a wall that turns, a TV preset and a set for several monitors), with a description, the parameters each address accepts (theme, density, refresh, scale, language, rotation, repository filter), a copy of the full address and the choice of opening it in a new window or in the same tab; for several monitors it opens one window per monitor with the layout chosen, placing each on its own monitor when the browser has the Window Management API and listing kiosk commands otherwise

- The catalogue of the Screens menu is also read by tools: `GET /api/screens/menu`, the new command `screens` of the command line (`screens list`, `screens urls`) and the MCP tool `screens_menu`; the three only read

- Five settings for the Screens menu: `screens.menu` (on by default; it only navigates), `screens.menu_target` (new window or same tab), `screens.menu_max_windows`, `screens.menu_rotate_seconds` and `screens.menu_tv_scale`, each with its help in Settings
- **An optional lint rule for a workflow with no `permissions:` block at the top of the file**: `permissions_top` (off until `lint.rules.permissions_top.enabled`, information by default, `lint.rules.permissions_top.severity`) names the files whose jobs set their own permissions while the file sets none, so a job added later would get the broader default token. It says nothing when the file has no permissions anywhere (the older `permissions` rule already does). The label, the help and the documentation exist in the eight languages.

- **The update of the page is tested end to end with real processes**: a test can point the updater of the page at a local zip (`MONITOR_TEST_HOOKS=1` with `MONITOR_TEST_UPDATE_ZIP`; without both nothing changes, and the zip goes through the same checks of contents, members and SHA-256 as any other), and new tests apply it in a second process, go back with the rollback and check that the version, the files and the data return as they were, that `--start` brings the new version up and checks its health, and that `--handover` waits for the running monitor to stop before it starts the new one.

- **The locks between processes are checked inside the container**: the container checks gain E9, which starts several processes of the image against one shared volume and counts what they write under the lock of the operating system and under the lock file, so a lost update would show in the evidence of the workflow; the test of the lock file now runs on Windows too.

- **A guide of its own for the assistant, `ASSISTANT.md`, in eight languages**: how to switch it on, its five fixed questions, the four tools it reads with, what it never does (it writes nothing, reaches nothing on GitHub, hands what a repository wrote over as data) and how to check it with the two routes and the record of prompts. The section of `AI_CONNECTION.md` stays as the reference of the settings.

- **Help with cost and effect on five more switches**: the tooltip of `ai.assistant.enabled`, `ai.prompt_log.enabled`, `ai.similar.enabled`, `ai.airgapped` and `api.v1_enabled` now says what you will see and what it costs, in the eight languages, beside the generic line every setting already had.

- **The record of prompts and the origin of the figures of a model, in the page**: under the AI in Settings, a card "Record of prompts" shows the last 20 entries (already cleaned of secrets and of your hidden words) and empties the record with a button, for an administrator on this computer; the table of models has a column "Origin" (the page, the file `config/ai_models.json` or the provider) so you can see where each figure comes from. Both in the eight languages.

- **Two more questions for the assistant, both read-only**: "the situation of a repository" (`explain_repo`, from its runs and its open alerts) and "what to do first" (`prioritize`, the open alerts, the status and the score put in order with their ids) join the three that existed; they use the same four tools, limits and daily budget of the AI, and there is still no free chat.

- **The example file of the settings carries 221 more settings**: the Settings page takes the default it shows from the example file, and 221 settings of the AI, the delivery and supply sentinels, the backups, the doctor, the pool of reads, the sounds, the webhook and the alerts were not in it, so the page showed no default for them. Each one now carries the value the code uses when the setting is absent; the register of surfaces lists the 61 that still lack it.

- **The 36 routes of the page that no guide named are in a table**: `API.md`, in the eight languages, ends with a table of the routes of the page that the contract does not freeze (the About panel, the guided runs with `act` and on GitHub, the actions of the AI, the analytics, the discovery, the help, the mode, the status bar, the logo and the rest), with what each one does and which ones only this computer may use; the register of surfaces no longer lists them as missing.

- **The progress of an update as server-sent events**: `GET /api/updates/stream` sends `hello`, one `progress` frame each time the progress file of the updater changes, keep-alive comments and `bye` when nothing runs any more (at most two listeners, 15 minutes each, read only, no new setting). The Updates menu uses it while an update runs and keeps asking every second and a half when the browser has no EventSource or the monitor restarts. Both skills list the route.
- **A note about where the trust of the SHA-256 rests**: the confirmation of an update now says that the zip and SHA256SUMS come from the same GitHub release, so the check finds a damaged or incomplete download but not a release replaced at its source; the trust rests on GitHub and on your account there. Also in the update guide.
- **`tools/compare_tree.py`, for whoever audits a package**: compares an extracted release (a folder or the zip) with the files a git ref versions, by content: line endings and the layout of JSON files do not count, a real change, a missing file and an extra file do (`COMPARE=SAME` or `DIFFERENT`, exit code 0, 1 or 2).
- **A browser test of the update dialog**: in English and Chinese the confirmation says what will happen and where the trust rests, and a person without the right to apply sees the reason and no button that applies.
- **One card per signal in the panels of Collaboration, Security, Delivery and Supply chain**: the same container as the CI sentinel (state lamp, counter, list, filter by repository and a line of help) for each of the 6, 6, 6 and 5 signals the sentinels raise; Delivery and Supply chain also have a table by code and filters by repository and level. A test compares the cards with the codes the sentinels really raise, in the eight languages.
- **A much richer Daily trend**: 18 series (stuck builds, share of green workflows, repositories with CI up to date, critical and high alerts, old Dependabot PRs, open issues, unsigned waiting, ready to release, average health, API allowance used, Actions minutes and the six that existed) from the history the sentinel already keeps, with no call to GitHub. Each card shows the value now, the change against yesterday and 7 days ago and a sparkline; the large chart has a range of 7, 30 or 60 days, a threshold, marks of monitor updates and incidents, a tooltip that also answers to the arrow keys, a second series to compare, a legend that switches the lines and CSV/JSON download. A day saved before a series existed is a gap, never a zero. Drawn in SVG, with dashes besides colour, for the light, dark and high-contrast themes and for phones.
- **The Daily trend gets the figures it did not have, release marks, thresholds in Settings, a bar view and a copy of the chart as an image**: seven new series from what the cycle already reads, with no call to GitHub (mean and p95 duration of the runs, mean wait in the queue, flaky workflows that failed and then passed on the same commit, size of the artifacts, and the medium and low alerts), the Actions minutes per day of the repositories that use most, a mark for the days a release was published, and the lines of the chart (share of green workflows 90, health 70, API allowance 80) as the settings `sentinel.trend_green_pct`, `sentinel.trend_health` and `sentinel.trend_quota_pct` with their tooltip. The chart can be lines or bars and can be copied as an image (or saved as a file when the browser does not allow copying). The brief of the tab has its own signal `trend.daily` with every series, its gaps and the lines, both skills name the new series, printing hides the controls and lets the whole tab flow onto the page, and the demo with data (`--rich`) fills the new series. A day without a figure is a gap, never a zero.
- **Both skills of the monitor describe the cards of the sentinel panels and the series of the daily trend**: a new reference lists the 23 alert codes behind the cards and the 18 series, how to read them through the briefs, the API and the command line, and the gaps of the history; a test fails when a card or a series is missing from either skill.
- **A browser test for the help of the settings**: the page probe focuses a switch and a number, checks that the tooltip appears inside the window, is tied to the control and goes away with Escape, in the wide and the narrow window.
- **The briefs for the AI tools reach the command line and the MCP server**: `monitor_cli.py briefs index` lists the catalogued briefs, `briefs show TAB` prints the signals of one tab and `briefs all` every brief in one document; the MCP server has the read-only tools `briefs_index` and `brief_tab` (a tab name or `all`). A test checks that the page, the HTTP routes, the command and the tools list the same tabs; nothing is written and the texts go through the same cleaner.
- **A workflow of its own that keeps the evidence an outside reader cannot produce**: the page walked in a browser in the eight languages at desktop and phone size (script errors, console errors and answers with a 5xx status), the updater from the download to the rollback against a false release server on this computer (intact zip, wrong hash, paths that leave the folder, cut or oversize downloads, a redirect to another host, two updaters at once and a failure injected in every phase), a double of GitHub with 250 and 727 repositories (403, 429 and lists that do not fit a page), hostile release notes, four processes writing the same file, and the container behind a trusted proxy. Each job keeps its output as an artifact.
- **Two more guards for what a reader cannot see**: the routes that only this computer may use are tried behind a real reverse proxy on the same machine (with the forwarding headers a proxy adds they are refused; a proxy that adds nothing looks local until the owner declares it, and the test says so), and the prompt that sets the monitor up with an AI must keep the seven rules, the steps, the questions and the keys it may write identical in the eight languages.
- **An optional assistant of fixed questions**: the Alerts tab can show a panel (off by default, `ai.assistant.enabled`, and it needs an AI provider) where the AI you configured explains an alert, summarises the situation or says why a repository is slow, only from the data of the monitor. It has a closed list of four tools that only read (`ai.assistant.tools`, at most `ai.assistant.max_tool_calls` readings per question), no free chat, shows the ids it read and how much it sent (`ai.assistant.max_context`), spends the one daily budget of the AI, hands over what a repository wrote as data between marks cut to `ai.untrusted_text_max`, lets a viewer ask and records each question in the log of actions (routes `GET /api/assistant` and `POST /api/assistant/ask`; not in the Lite edition).
- **The read API for clients switches on by itself in the service mode**: `api.v1_enabled` is now `auto` (the default), `on` or `off`. With `auto` the prefix `/api/v1/` answers when the monitor runs as a service (an account of the team mode, the Docker image, the systemd unit) and stays closed for one person at one computer; the old true and false keep their meaning. `/api/v1/me` also tells the version of the contract (`contract`) and what the session may do (`capabilities`).
- **An air-gapped mode of the AI that can be proved**: with `ai.airgapped` on (off by default) the AI only talks to a model on this computer or on a private address you list in `ai.airgap_hosts`; any address of the internet and any cloud service is refused before a connection is opened, in the chat, the list of models, the test of a model, the fallback chain, the assistant and the summaries, and the AI doctor adds the check `ai_egress`. The general `airgapped` switch turns it on too and the setting can only be changed from the computer of the monitor. `ai.redact.extra_terms` adds words of yours that are always hidden before the AI sees a text.
- **Alerts that look alike, without a model**: with `ai.similar.enabled` on (off by default) "explain this alert" also lists up to `ai.similar.max` alerts that look like it, among the open ones and the resolved ones the feed remembers, found by a plain comparison of words (no embeddings), only from the data of the account and cited by id.
- **The complete OpenAPI document of the read API**: `/api/v1/openapi.json` now describes every frozen route (55 paths) with the keys and types of the contract, built from `docs/schemas/api_contract.json` and not by hand; the routes the contract does not freeze are experimental, answer 404 under `/api/v1/` and are listed only when the new setting `api.experimental_routes` is on. A test keeps the document, the contract and the routes of the code in agreement.
- **A contract suite for outside clients**: `tools/api_contract_suite.py` (only the standard library, only reads) checks any server that speaks the read API against the document it serves: the shape of every route, the version header, the error envelope and its stable codes, the pages, gzip and that nothing can be written. `monitor_cli.py api contract-check` runs it, and the CI runs it against the demo in the service mode; a test proves that it fails, naming the check, against a server that breaks the contract.
- **Every answer under `/api/v1/` carries the header `X-API-Version`**: the three own routes (`me`, `repos`, `openapi.json`) and the routes written by their own handler (for example `data` and `auth/me`) did not say it; the header is now added in one place for every answer of the prefix, errors included, and the contract suite checks it.
- **The command line asks the frozen routes under `/api/v1/`**: `monitor_cli.py` is now a client of the read API: it asks the routes the contract freezes under the prefix and falls back to the old route when the monitor answers 404 there (and does not try again), so no command changes its output. New setting `cli.prefer_v1` (on by default) and the variable `MONITOR_CLI_PREFER_V1`. A test checks that the same commands give the same JSON by both ways and that every route the command line, the terminal views and the MCP server read is frozen in the contract or written down with its reason.
- **Examples with curl for the read API**: the guide of the API shows how to read `/api/v1/me`, page through `/api/v1/repos`, download `/api/v1/openapi.json` and follow the cursor, in the eight languages, and a test runs the commands it shows against a monitor.
- **Patterns of your own for what the AI must never see, with an engine that cannot hang**: `ai.redact.extra_patterns` (off, empty) takes patterns of whole words with three wildcards (`*`, `?`, `#`, for example `deploy-*`); they are not regular expressions, a pattern is 3 to 80 characters, a word is read up to 2000 characters and a text spends a fixed number of steps after which the rest is hidden. They apply to every text that reaches a model, the active notice included (which now also hides `ai.redact.extra_terms`). A test feeds hostile texts to the engine and checks the time, and another one runs the assistant, the notice and the summary of the report at the same moment against the one daily budget of the AI.
- **An optional local record of prompts**: with `ai.prompt_log.enabled` on (off by default) every question sent to the AI and its answer, already cleaned of secrets and of your hidden words, are written to `ai_prompts.jsonl` in the folder of the logs, up to `ai.prompt_log.keep` lines; an administrator reads them with `GET /api/ai/prompts` and empties them with `POST /api/ai/prompts/clear`. A cached answer is not written, the system prompt is not kept, and the file is not in the backup, the zip or the files that AI tools read.
- **A table of models you keep by hand, and a list of retired models**: `config/ai_models.json` (it does not exist until you write it) takes rows with the context, the prices, the tools and the daily limit of a model that your provider does not tell, which count above the provider's figures and below what you typed on the page, and a list of `retired` models that the chain of fallbacks skips even with the live list of models off; the AI doctor names a retired model with the one to use instead. A wrong row is left out and named, nothing else changes.
- **The read API for clients is also a set of tools of the MCP server**: `api_me`, `api_openapi` (the document, or one route with `path`) and `api_contract_check` (the contract suite) give the same documents as `GET /api/v1/me`, `GET /api/v1/openapi.json` and `monitor_cli.py api contract-check`, read only and with no flag; a test compares the three doors, and the command line and the MCP server now load the contract suite through one function.
- **A step by step guide of the read API, `API_V1.md`, in eight languages**: how to know whether the API is open, how to identify yourself, the commands in order (who you are, the document, the pages with filters, `fields`, `profile=compact` and the cursor, gzip, health and alerts), the errors and their stable codes, the same three things from the command line and the MCP server, and how to check a server before relying on it. A test runs every command of the guide against a monitor.
- **`check_api_contract.py --v1` is the whole contract in one command**: besides the frozen shapes of the routes of the prefix it now runs the contract suite for clients (`api_contract_suite.py`) and prints one line per failed check, so the old tool and the suite no longer say different things; the checks are not written twice.
- **The register of surfaces covers the settings, the routes, the commands and the MCP tools, not only the tabs**: for each of the 706 settings, each audited, exempt or frozen route, each command of the command line and each tool of the MCP server the test checks the help, the words in the eight languages, the guide, the place in the skills and the example file, and fails when something is missing that no exception names with the version that closes it (the file of exceptions lists what is still to do for 2.1.5: the routes the guides do not name yet and the settings missing from the example). Closing the gaps it found, the guide of the command line now names `lessons`, `briefs`, `credential`, `install-skill` and `gh-token` in the eight languages, the help of the command line names `team`, `compare`, `credential` and `update`, and the skill for outside AIs lists every tool of the MCP server.

### Fixed

- **The workflow checker never calls something it did not read clean**: a folder or file you name that does not exist now says so on the error output and exits with 2 (before, it printed `0 finding(s).` and exited 0), and a workflow file that cannot be read is named while the others are still checked and the exit code is 2 (before, it was skipped without a word). The `--remote` behaviour is unchanged.

- **Two updaters at once no longer step on each other's download**: each call downloads into a folder of its own (removed when it ends); before, both wrote the same SHA256SUMS and one of them could read it half written and refuse with "publishes no SHA-256".
- **A lock file its owner could not delete no longer blocks for five minutes on Windows**: when the delete is refused because a reader has the file open, the owner now writes a released mark inside it and the next process takes it over after a second; `os_lock` also waits at least 45 s on Windows (when asked for 5 s or more) and polls with a little jitter, so a loaded machine no longer gives up on a live holder.
- **A flaky browser test of the history count**: the probe waited a fixed 1.5 s for the data and failed on a loaded Windows machine; it now waits until the data is there.
- **The cleaner of text no longer slows down with long texts**: five thousand characters of letters, dots, digits or dashes took between two and six seconds because several patterns tried to match from every position; the prefixes and addresses now have the length that real names have, the time grows with the size of the text and a test bounds it. Secrets after a long run are still found.
- **Two updaters started at the same moment no longer end in an error**: the lock of the update is now written whole and put in place in one step, so the second updater is told the update is locked instead of failing with an exception, and a lock that was still being written is no longer taken for an old one.
- **An update that fails before anything was touched is reported as such**: a download that is cut, a full disk or a damaged zip while the plan is made is now an answer (code 4) and not an exception, and a failure in the step that copies the installation no longer ends as a failed rollback when there was nothing to put back.
- **The walk through the page no longer calls a short tab empty in Japanese and Chinese**: a tab with a line or two of text, which says the same in a third of the characters, was reported as drawing almost nothing; those two languages now need fewer characters.
- **The GitHub App no longer loses its larger allowance in silence**: when the monitor was started without `openssl` in the PATH it could not sign the token request and went back to the token of the user (5,000 calls an hour instead of the 10,000 or more of the App) without saying anything. The monitor now signs by itself with the standard library (keys PKCS#1 and PKCS#8 without passphrase, proved against `openssl`), keeps `openssl` only as a fallback, and when the App is on but not used it says so: the doctor turns amber with the cause (key unreadable, signature failed, App not installed, token refused), the failure is noted once, and the Quota tab shows a `GitHub App` line with the cause and the limit in use (`app` in `/api/quota`, `signer` and `code` in `/api/github-app/status`).

## [2.1.3]

### Changed

- **The pictures of the README are taken at 1080p and show the newest tabs**: `tools/make_screenshots.py` photographs every page at 1920x1080 by default (`--viewport WIDTHxHEIGHT` changes it; the 4K, ultrawide, portrait, phone and wall-screen pictures keep their own size), `tools/demo_monitor.py --rich` gives the demo findings for the Delivery and Supply chain sentinels, a daily trend and a comparison of periods, and the README (in the eight languages) shows the Delivery, Supply chain, Daily trend, Compare periods and Updates pages.
- **Yesterday vs today has a menu entry of its own**: the comparison with the last saved report of the Sentinel moved out of the summary into its own tab (address `#sentinel-yesterday`, next to the Daily trend), so the report of the Sentinel takes the whole page; the entry follows the CI sentinel, has its help, its text in the eight languages and the same look as the other tabs of the Sentinel.

### Security

- **The Updates menu is harder to abuse**: five refused or failed attempts to update or go back inside ten minutes pause the buttons for ten minutes (the answer is `cooldown`, with its text in the eight languages), the status says why an update is blocked (`blocked_reason`, `cooldown_seconds`), the progress file of the updater is read only when it is a plain file of a reasonable size (a link, a huge file or a truncated or hostile one counts as no update running), the log of the updater is closed once the process has started, and the workflow linter that feeds the Supply chain and Delivery sentinels cuts a file at 1 MiB, 20000 lines and 4000 characters a line, so an endless line or a million lines cannot hold the cycle. Tests refuse a foreign Host or Origin on every route of the menu.

### Added

- **A tooltip on every setting**: pointing at a setting, giving it the keyboard focus or pressing and holding on a touch screen shows what it does, its range or values and its default, what happens when a switch is turned on and off, and whether it applies at once or after a restart; the settings that cost quota or add a page (the comparison of periods, the check for updates, the wall screens) also say what you will see and what it costs. The help text of every setting is checked in the eight languages and an entry for a setting that does not exist is refused.
- **Two guards so that nothing grows alone**: the guides must have the same headings and blocks of code in the eight languages, and a single register of the tabs of the page checks for each one its button, its name in the eight languages, its help topic, its line in the guide of the AIs, its row in the table of signals and its place in the lite edition; a new tab that is not registered, or a missing piece without a version that closes it, fails the test.

### Fixed

- **Folding panels of the Sentinel tabs no longer paint one over another**: when the panels that stay folded filled the box, the one panel unfolded on its own was squeezed to a few pixels and its body was drawn over the panels below it. It now keeps a minimum height and the box scrolls instead, for all the Sentinel tabs. The browser test unfolds every panel of every Sentinel tab one by one, all together, by hand and in turns, wide and narrow, and compares their rectangles.

## [2.1.2]

### Fixed

- **The unfolded panel of a Sentinel tab reaches the bottom of its box**: in the CI, collaboration and security tabs the one panel that is unfolded was cut at 46% of the height of the window and left an empty strip below it; it now takes the rest of the box and scrolls inside when its content is taller. The rule is measured in a headless browser in the eight languages, wide and narrow (`tools/check_unfolded.py`).
- **Pressing a counter of the header no longer leaves Now, the alerts and the other menus cut down until the page is reloaded**: the counters of red workflows and of unsigned builds jump to the sentinel together with a filter of repositories, and that filter stayed on for every tab; leaving the tab they jumped to now takes it away (the chip of the filter still clears it by hand), and the browser test presses every counter, goes back to Now and walks through all the tabs looking for a cut list, an empty tab or an error of the page.
- **Starting the monitor from the updater no longer opens a console window for every git and gh call**: the process started after an update had no console at all, so each command it ran opened a visible one. It now starts with a hidden console that its children share, and so do the processes of the account supervisor; a test reads every place that starts a process and refuses the detached flag.

### Added

- **Comparison of periods (setting `compare.enabled`, off by default)**: this period against the one before (7 or 30 days or any range, the same dates a year ago or a baseline of your own) with a filter for workdays or weekends in the time zone of the monitor. It sets the share of failed runs, the median duration, the time to resolve alerts, the mean score and its coverage, each with its change and its direction; a figure without enough data or without a baseline says `insufficient_data` or `baseline_unavailable` instead of showing a 0 or a 100. It is read only, made from the ledger and the snapshots of the score, and asks nothing of GitHub. While it is on, the ledger of the score keeps two periods of `compare.default_window_days`. Guide COMPARE.md.
- **What changed between the two periods**: the repositories that got worse or better, the workflows that started or stopped failing and the alerts opened, in `GET /api/compare` (with `format=ndjson` and `format=csv`), in `monitor_cli.py compare` (exit 4 when a figure cannot be compared, 5 with `--fail-on` when one got worse) and in a section of the Charts tab.
- **A short comparison in the scheduled report (setting `compare.include_in_report`, off by default)**: one line per figure against the period before, in both formats and the eight languages.
- **The updater is now a menu of the monitor (System > Updates, Complete edition)**: the page shows the installed version and the one the check found (setting `updates.check`: with it off, or the monitor offline, nothing is asked of GitHub), lists the published versions with their notes when you press "Look for versions", and updates to the latest or to a chosen version after a confirmation that says what will happen: the SHA-256 of the zip against SHA256SUMS, the copy made first (with its folder), the migration of the configuration and the restart. The update runs in a process of its own: the monitor stops by itself, the update is applied, the monitor starts again and the page reconnects; if the new version does not start, the previous one is put back and started. "Go back to the version before" does the same in reverse. Only an administrator at the computer where the monitor runs can apply; one update at a time; every step goes to the log of actions; it is not offered in the Lite edition, in a container or under a service manager. The routes are `/api/updates/status`, `progress`, `history`, `check`, `plan`, `apply` and `rollback`; the stand-alone `tools/monitor_updater.py` stays as the way back when the monitor does not start and shares the same code.
- **The updater has two new options**: `--handover` waits for the monitor that started it to stop, applies the update and starts the monitor again (a failed start brings the previous version back and starts it), and `--status-file` writes the progress to a JSON file that the page reads, also after the restart.
- **The Delivery and Supply chain sentinels have a tab of their own**: like the CI, collaboration and security ones, each has its entry in the Sentinel group of the menu (`#sentinel-delivery` and `#sentinel-supply`), a panel with the open findings of each repository, the state of its cycle and what could not be read, a badge with the errors, its help topic and its texts in the eight languages.
- **The daily trend has a menu entry of its own and fits its frame**: the small curves of the summary of the sentinel moved to their own tab (`#sentinel-trend`, opened from a button of the summary, which keeps only its own panels), and every curve and figure now adapts to the width of its frame instead of overflowing or being cut, on a phone and on the wall screens too.
- **A prompt to set up the monitor with an AI (`docs/AI_SETUP_PROMPT.md`, in the eight languages)**: a text to paste into an AI assistant that installs the monitor from the zip after checking its SHA-256, asks one question at a time about the account, the repositories, the sentinels, the quota, the notifications, the screens and the AI, writes only settings that exist in the template, keeps every secret out of the chat and the file, leaves everything that changes things switched off, and ends by explaining how the installation will look and how to update or remove it. It travels in the package and a test checks that each setting it names exists.

### Changed

- **The files that several processes write are guarded by the lock of the operating system**: the log of actions, the state and the feed of the AI channel, the notification ledger, the dispatcher, the lessons, the schedule, the report state and the storage of the accounts take a lock that the system lets go when its owner dies, so a process that was killed can no longer leave one of them locked until the marker file gets old; the old marker files, if any, are inert.
- **The architecture picture shows the five sentinels, the comparison of periods and the Updates menu.**

## [2.1.1]

### Fixed

- **The release gate accepts a green run by the count unittest itself gave**: the record of a complete green run carries the number of tests that unittest says it ran (the lines of the report can be fewer when a test prints in the middle of its line), and the gate now compares that number with the total instead of the number of lines it could read, so a complete run is no longer refused for a few uncounted lines.
- **A clock set back no longer keeps the webhook coverage live**: an event marked ahead of the clock is not believed, so the indicator goes back to waiting instead of showing live until the clock catches up, and the savings that depend on a live webhook are not kept on by it.

### Added

- **`make_release.py --progress` says how far the suites are**: with the flag, both the ordinary suite and the suite of the clean install print one line every 250 tests, so the gate no longer stays silent for the better part of an hour.
- **The Supply chain sentinel (Complete only, off by default)**: the fourth sentinel reads, without writing anything to GitHub, the workflow files of each repository through the API (no clone; a file whose SHA did not change is not read again) and raises one alert per repository and signal: `actions.supply.unpinned` (a `uses:` not pinned to a full commit), `actions.supply.mutable_ref` (it follows `@main` or `@master`), `actions.supply.permissions_wide` (`write-all`), `actions.supply.permissions_missing` (no `permissions:` block) and `actions.supply.pr_target_checkout` (`pull_request_target` with a checkout of the pull request). The findings come from the workflow linter (no rules of its own, the rule that looks for secrets stays off so the value of a secret is never read), each signal has its own switch and level, a repository whose workflows cannot be read is listed under `unavailable` and never counted as clean, and a restart or the first look announces nothing. The cost is bounded by `max_repos_per_tick`, `max_calls_per_tick` and `min_rate_remaining`. New: `sentinels.supply.*` settings in the Sentinel sub-tab, `GET /api/sentinel/supply`, `alerts --sentinel supply`, the metrics `supply_*`, and `docs/SENTINEL_SUPPLY.md` in the 8 languages.

- **The Delivery sentinel (Complete only, off by default)**: the fifth sentinel reads, without writing anything to GitHub, the latest releases, the latest tags, the CHANGELOG and the version files you name of each repository through the API (no clone) and raises one alert per repository and signal: `release.unreleased_pending` (entries wait under `[Unreleased]` while the latest published release is older than `unreleased_days`), `release.tag_without_release` (a version tag newer than the latest release has none of its own), `release.release_without_notes` (the latest release has an empty description), `release.version_mismatch` (the version files you list and the CHANGELOG do not say the same version), `release.ecosystem_drift` (a repository pins an older version than the latest release of the provider of a shared contract, read from the pin tables of the readiness settings) and `release.assets_missing` (the latest release lacks a file for one of the `expected_assets` patterns). Each signal has its own switch and level, a signal that is off is not read, a repository that cannot be read is listed under `unavailable` and never counted as clean, a restart or the first look announces nothing, and the score does not use it. The cost is bounded by `max_repos_per_tick`, `max_calls_per_tick` and `min_rate_remaining`. New: `sentinels.delivery.*` settings in the Sentinel sub-tab, the events `delivery_new` and `delivery_fixed` (family releases, off in every default list), `GET /api/sentinel/delivery`, `alerts --sentinel delivery`, the metrics `delivery_*`, and `docs/SENTINEL_DELIVERY.md` in the 8 languages.

### Changed

- **The screenshots of the README show the current version**: every picture of the page, the wall screens and the console tools was taken again with invented data. New ones show the menu in titled groups, the sentinels of CI and collaboration, the fleet, the reports, the whole page in another theme and the wall screens with the logo of an organization, in other themes and turning by itself; the About panel is taller so that the operation and backup card is visible.
- **An address that asks for no first-run wizard also hides the one-time notice about the budget of the AI**, so that a picture taken from a clean browser shows the page at rest.
- **The doctor says in red that the incoming webhook has no secret**: with the receiver on and no shared secret set, every delivery is refused with `401` and the doctor now reports a failure instead of a warning; the webhook guide says that the secret is the only barrier.
- **The release gate compares the content of each file of the zip with the commit**: besides the names and a clean tree, every member is compared with the file the commit versions (line endings aside), so a file that differs from the commit cannot travel.

## [2.1.0]

### Fixed

- **The webhook through the supervisor of the accounts answered 400 to every delivery**: the process of an account, when the supervisor forwards a delivery to it, could not tell who sent it because a standard module was used without being imported, so a valid signature, a wrong one, a missing one, a repeated delivery and a large body all got the same generic answer. The sender is read again as before (a valid key of the supervisor gives the address it reports, any other falls back to the peer), and a test now looks through the whole package for a standard module used in a function without being imported.
- **The status label of a detail panel stays on one line**: in the Sentinel tab the label (for example the one that says something must be done) in the heading of each panel of Detail by layer was squeezed to one letter per line, in every language and especially on a phone. It no longer shrinks or breaks inside a word, and a test in a real browser measures it in the eight languages, wide and narrow.
- **The container guide tells how to open the first session and which image tags exist**: it asked to open the address with the access token, which the monitor has refused by default since the access token stopped travelling in the address. It now says to send the token with a POST or the access header (or to turn the address on for one visit and off again), and the tags it quotes are those of this version.

### Added

- **A complete backup of the installation, verified and optionally sealed (`backup create`)**: `monitor_cli.py backup create` writes one package (format 2) with the dump of every account, the cleaned settings and lists of the installation and a manifest with the size and the SHA-256 of every member; it is verified before it is put in place and a package of the first format still verifies and restores. Tokens, keys, passwords, the value of any `*_env` variable, the webhook secret, the sessions and the content of the secret store never travel: the settings go through a white list and then through a scrub of anything that looks like a secret. With `ops.backup.encrypt` (or `--encrypt`) the package is sealed with AES-256-GCM using a key from the variable named by `ops.backup.key_env`; it needs the optional library `cryptography` and, without it or without a key of 12 characters or more, the copy is refused with a clear message and nothing is written. `backup verify` (with `--deep` it restores into a temporary folder), `backup list` and `backup status` complete it.
- **Restore by account or into a folder, with a checklist (`backup restore`)**: a restore verifies the package first, is refused while a monitor answers on its port and into a storage that already holds accounts unless `--force`, merges the settings (a key the package does not carry is never removed), can restore one account alone (`--account`) or an empty folder (`--to-dir`), and writes a report with what is left to do (the login of GitHub, the settings left out, the restart, the doctor).
- **A scheduled copy, a status and a card in the About panel (`ops.backup.enabled`)**: off by default, the monitor makes a backup every `ops.backup.interval_hours` in a thread of its own, keeps the newest `ops.backup.keep` and records the result; a failure is written down and never stops the monitor. `GET /api/ops/status` and `POST /api/ops/backup` (an administrator, from this computer) and the **Operation** card of the About panel show and run it. `deploy/github-actions-monitor-backup.service` with its timer make the same copy under systemd, and `deploy/compose.yaml` keeps the copies in a volume of their own. The guide `docs/OPS.md` explains it all in eight languages.
- **The doctor looks at the operation and exits with codes that do not change (`doctor --strict`)**: the Complete edition checks that the data, logs and backup folders really accept a file, that a running monitor and its supervisor are healthy, the webhook secret by length only, the integrity of every SQLite database read only and with a time limit (`quick_check` by default), the age of the last copy, the free disk and `/metrics`. It exits `0` when all is well, `1` when a check failed, `3` for warnings with `--strict` (or `ops.doctor.strict_exit`) and `4` when it could not run. The lite edition does not run or load any of it.
- **A purge on demand that does not exist until you switch it on (`ops.purge.enabled`)**: `POST /api/ops/purge` and the button of the About panel run the retention purge now, are a dry run unless asked, and are refused without a good backup newer than `ops.purge.require_backup_hours`; the line goes to the audit of the retention and to the log of actions.
- **A backup package of one account (`backup create --account`)**: it holds that account alone, with no user, no session and none of the settings or lists of the installation; `backup verify` checks that it holds that account alone and `backup restore` restores it without touching the other accounts, the users or the files of the destination, and is refused only when that account already exists there and there is no `--force`.
- **The whole product wears the theme chosen in Settings, and each wall screen can have its own**: the theme (`ui.theme`: auto, light, dark or one of the sixteen) is also kept by the monitor, so the wall screens and the sign-in page wear it. The screens inherit it by default (`screens.default_theme` is now `inherit`), and `screens.default_theme`, `screens.theme_overview`, `screens.theme_now` and `screens.theme_sentinels` fix another one for all of them or for one. No wall screen writes a colour of its own any more: they use the palettes of the page, so the sixteen themes work there too.
- **A logo of the organization on the wall screens**: uploaded in Settings (PNG, JPEG, WebP or SVG, at most 1 MB; the type is checked by the first bytes of the file and an SVG with scripts, event handlers or links to other files is refused) and kept in the data folder, never in the configuration. `screens.logo_overview`, `screens.logo_now` and `screens.logo_sentinels` choose the screens (only the overview by default), `screens.logo_corner` the corner (top-right by default) and `screens.logo_max_width` and `screens.logo_max_height` the largest size (160 x 64 pixels by default). The picture is fitted without changing its shape and has a place of its own, so it never covers the content.
- **One wall screen that rotates and jumps to a new red (`screens.mode` = rotate)**: instead of three separate screens, one window turns through overview, right now and sentinels every `screens.rotate_every_seconds` seconds (20 by default, 5 to 300). A new red jumps to the screen that shows it (Right now for a workflow that failed, Sentinels for a panel in red) and stays `screens.alert_hold_seconds` seconds (30 by default, 5 to 600) or until the red is gone, whichever comes first, and then goes on turning; a red already shown does not make it jump again until it changes or comes back. `?jump=off` and `?rotate=0` calm one window. The default stays three separate screens.
- **The overview wall screen shows the map of the fleet by groups**: under the four tiles of the fleet, one bar for each group of the Fleet view (the same grouping: ecosystem, label or state, from `fleet.group_by`) with how many of its repositories are red, need attention, are in order or have no data. It carries only the name of the group and numbers, never the name of a repository, and follows the theme and the language of the screen. Setting `screens.group_map` (on by default).
- **A failing "Dependabot Updates" is an alert of its own, and the activity of Copilot is information**: the failed run of the dependency updates that GitHub makes for itself becomes an alert with medium severity, one per repository (`ci.dependabot_failed`, switch `ci.dependabot_alerts`, on by default), and a failed Copilot workflow can become a notice (`ci.copilot_activity`, switch `ci.copilot_alerts`, off by default). Neither is ever a red of the repository, both can be silenced like any alert.
- **The beep of this computer has the kinds of event of the page, and the live view that comes back has its own sound**: with `sound.server_beep` the monitor now sounds one pattern for each kind (a new red, a lost connection, a warning, a solved one, a security alert, a stuck run, a drop of the score, a notice, a report and a lesson) by the same switches and quiet hours as the page, in one burst of at most three. A new sound, four rising notes, tells that the connection is back after a loss, in the page, in the wall screens and on this computer. Setting `sound.on_live_recovered` (on by default).
- **An updater that moves an installation to any published version and migrates its configuration (`monitor_updater.py`)**: one file with only the standard library, published next to every release (with its SHA-256) and also inside the product (`tools/monitor_updater.py`) and as the command `monitor_cli.py update`. It detects the installation, lists every version published on GitHub, lets you choose the target, shows a dry run of what changes in your configuration, applies it with a copy first, the SHA-256 checked, a safe unpacking, your own launcher lines kept and an automatic return if anything fails, and keeps a history. It opens as a page in the browser, a window (Tkinter) or a text menu. The registry of migrations (`assets/migrations/`, built by `tools/make_migrations.py`) covers every release from 1.8.1. See `docs/UPDATE.md`.

### Changed

- **`tools/backup_storage.py` writes the package of the product**: its backups are now the package of `backup create` (format 2, the data of the accounts with their settings cleaned of secrets) and it takes `--account`; the backups of the first format still verify and restore with it.
- **The release gate says WHICH test failed, keeps the whole output and can accept a green run it has already seen**: a failed suite (the ordinary one or the one of the extracted zip) now names the failing tests and saves its complete output (`MONITOR_RELEASE_LOG_DIR`, else the temporary folder) instead of showing only the last lines, which were usually warnings. `tools/make_release.py --accept-green-suite RECORD --commit SHA` takes the record of a complete green run of that very commit (finished, no failure, no error, every test run) in place of running the ordinary suite again, and `--skip-clean-suite` also skips the suite of the extracted zip while the smoke checks of the clean install still run and the gate says so; a record of another commit, an unfinished one or one with a failure is refused.

### Security

- **The links the page builds from data are checked by reading the address, not by its first letters**: only an https address of GitHub (or of the host you set for GitHub Enterprise, which may also be http) with no user name or password passes; a script address, a look-alike host, a blank, a control character or a backslash turns into an empty link.

## [2.0.9]

### Security

- **The access token no longer travels in the address by default (`listen.query_access` is now off)**: in the network mode the `?access=` address opened the session, but the token stayed in the history, the logs and the `Referer`. The setting is now off unless you turn it on (the default, the example file and the Settings help say so); the session is opened by `POST /api/access` (the token in the body) or by the header `X-Monitor-Access`. An installation that wrote `listen.query_access` itself keeps its value; one that relied on the old default and opens the monitor from an address with `?access=` has to turn the setting on, or use `POST /api/access`. The environment variable `MONITOR_QUERY_ACCESS=1` turns it on for a service.
- **A silence by pattern of a repository no longer silences what is not a workflow**: a pattern with only a repository (`alerts.silences`) kept quiet the notice of a new security alert (secret scanning, CodeQL, Dependabot), of a required check in red, of a pending signature and of a release notice of that repository, and the Alerts tab did not show them as silenced. A silence now covers only the red, fixed, stuck and failed notices of a workflow, the channels and the tab share the same list, and the help says so.
- **The cleaner of text that leaves the monitor catches more forms of a real log and does not touch what is not a secret**: Windows paths written with doubled bars (how an error message or JSON writes them), names with `_PASS`, `docker login -p`, `sshpass -p`, `curl -u`, `.netrc`, `.pgpass`, `_auth=` and `X-Auth`, passwords in Spanish, tokens of Hugging Face, Docker Hub, Stripe and Google OAuth, Telegram tokens without the word bot, passwords with an at sign in an address, session identifiers in a query, ntfy topics, hidden e-mail addresses and names that only exist in a private network are now cleaned, and a secret that only shows once the text is plain is cleaned even when the text also has a decoy. The counters, states and names that look like a secret (`max_tokens`, `secret_scanning: disabled`, `cache-key`, a repository called secret-santa, a version of four numbers) are no longer replaced, and two keys that look the same once cleaned are both kept.
- **A header that carries a secret never follows a redirect**: the token of an ntfy topic on a server that is not this computer and the local hook no longer send it on to the address a redirect points to; the redirect ends as an error.
- **What a webhook delivery may say is checked**: the status, the conclusion and the dates must have the shapes GitHub uses, and the names of the workflow, the branch, the job and the steps are kept in one line and cleaned like any text that leaves the monitor. A date that is not a date can no longer stop a cycle.
- **A provider on the internet only enters the chain of fallbacks if you allow it**: the text of a failed build is no longer sent to a cloud provider written in `ai.fallback` unless its name is in `ai.fallback_cloud_allowed` (empty by default). Every time the chain goes from this computer to the internet it is written in the log of actions and a notice is sent.
- **An https address of an AI provider on a private address needs its own switch** (`ai.allow_private_targets`, off by default): an address that points to a private, local-network or metadata address is refused, as is one with a user or an at sign before the host. If you use a server of your own network, turn the switch on.
- **A webhook payload has to name its owner, and its link has to point to its repository**: a delivery without the full name of the repository is no longer believed, and the link of the run that goes to the notices, the live list and the stream is kept only when it is under that owner and repository.

### Added


- **`logs.failed_step.max_per_minute` limits how many different runs the log of the failed step reads from GitHub in a minute** (10 by default): asking for many runs one after another no longer spends your allowance on all of them.
- **Silences by pattern can expire, and a signal lists the ones that need a decision** (`alerts.silences_expire_days`, 0 = never by default): with a number, a silence stops working that many days after its review date; the reminders already made are kept in the state file, so a restart does not repeat them; and the signal of the silences lists the ones past their date, expired or without a date.
- **The reds that aged into inactive are listed in the view of what is happening now**: a red that nothing of the events that decide has touched for the number of days of the age (30 by default) still leaves the red list, but now a line says how many there are and a list of them (repository, workflow and date of the failure) opens from it, so a failure never disappears without a trace.
- **The inactive workflows are listed in the view of what is happening now as well**: a workflow that failed and has not run for the days of ci.inactive_days (90 by default) no longer counts as red, and a second line says how many there are and opens their list, next to the one for the aged reds.
- **The page says once that the budget of the AI changed**: the first time this version is opened a bar explains that the daily limit and the limits of a model now also count the active notice; "Understood" hides it and the page remembers it.
- **The menu on the left has titled groups, and Team and Sentinel are split into one entry each**: five groups (Watch, Sentinels, Team, Reports and learning, System; they fold and are remembered) classify every tab; *Team* becomes Team health (`#team-health`), Fleet (`#fleet`) and Scheduled reports (`#team-reports`), and the *Sentinel* tab becomes a Summary (`#sentinel`) plus one entry for the CI sentinel (`#sentinel-ci`, with the panels A1 to A13), the Collab sentinel (`#sentinel-collab`) and the Security sentinel (`#sentinel-security`). The entry of a sentinel is in the menu only while that sentinel is on (`sentinel.enabled`, `sentinels.collab.enabled`, `sentinels.security.enabled`); its address still opens a panel that says it is off and opens its switch in Settings; each badge counts only the alerts of its own sentinel. The old addresses (`#team`, `#sent`, `#team/fleet`, `?view=team`) still open the right panel, each tab opens its own help topic, and `ui.screen1_tabs` lists the new tabs (`fleet`, `trep`, `sci`, `scol`, `ssec`).
- **One sound for each kind of event, with a theme, switches per kind and quiet hours**: a new red, a serious or security alert, a stuck build or queue, a drop of the score, a lost connection, a warning, something solved, a new report, a new lesson and a plain notice each have their own pattern of tones, made with the Web Audio API (no audio files, nothing leaves the browser), so you can tell by ear what happened. The settings `sound.on_warning`, `sound.on_resolved`, `sound.on_report`, `sound.on_lesson`, `sound.on_sentinel`, `sound.on_stuck`, `sound.on_score_drop` and `sound.on_info` join the two that existed (all on by default), `sound.theme` chooses `classic` or `soft` tones, and `sound.quiet_enabled` with `sound.quiet_from_hour` and `sound.quiet_to_hour` set quiet hours (off by default). In Settings, Interface, a button per kind plays it with the volume and theme in use. The wall screens use the same sounds (`screens.sound` and `?sound=on` still decide whether a window sounds). `docs/SOUNDS.md` has the table of sounds in eight languages.
- **Read only commands from Slack and Discord (setting `chatops.enabled`, off by default)**: people in an allowed channel can ask the monitor for `help`, `status`, `alerts`, `red`, `queue` or `score` and get a short answer that only they see, with no mention and, when `chatops.links` is on (the default), no link but the address of a repository next to a name shown as it is. `chatops.show_repo_names` is `all` by default (private repositories included): the names reach every channel and user your lists allow, which is why the lists are required and must all match; `public` and `none` hide them. The list of commands is closed (`chatops.allowed_words` can only narrow it) and every command only reads what the monitor already holds: none writes on GitHub, runs an action, changes a setting or reads a secret. `POST /api/chatops/slack` checks the signature `v0` of Slack (HMAC-SHA256 of the time and the body, at most five minutes old, the secret only in the environment variable named by `chatops.slack.signing_secret_env`); `POST /api/chatops/discord` checks the Ed25519 signature of the interaction with the public key named by `chatops.discord.public_key_env`; both answer the challenge of the platform, never accept a signature twice, refuse a body over 16 KB and count the requests per address, user, channel and minute. The lists `chatops.allowed_teams`, `chatops.allowed_channels` and `chatops.allowed_users` are required (with none, no command runs), every outside text is cleaned, cut and escaped, and each command leaves a line in the log of actions with the ids of the chat and never the text. A block in the Webhook tab of Settings, a guide in eight languages (`docs/CHATOPS.md`) and tests with the vectors of RFC 8032.

### Changed

- **The line of a cascade in the hook is rebuilt field by field** and the name of a workflow it hands to an AI is cut to 40 characters, like the alerts of their own: a name written by anybody with write access can no longer carry a sentence through the list of the followers.
- **The integrated AI cleans its answers more and believes less of what a provider says about itself**: links of any active scheme, HTML tags and terminal codes are removed from an answer; the tokens a provider says it spent are believed up to ten million (a mistake no longer shuts the AI for the day); the table of data per model keeps at most 500 rows, the ones with figures typed by hand first, and refuses a provider label that is not a host name or a preset.
- **`ai.base_url` refuses an address with a user or an at sign before the host**, so a key cannot be saved inside the address or hidden behind a host that looks like another.
- **The reminder of a review date is among the events of a new installation** (`notify.events`), and the help of the limits of the AI says that the active notice has its own limits per hour. The guide of the webhook now says what the code does: the run keeps its kind of event, the repository is read again in the next cycle, the names kept, and that the list of live events is read with the access token in the network mode.
- **One budget of the AI that also counts the active notice**: the daily total (`ai.daily_limit`), the limit and the cost of a model now include the calls of the active notice, which keeps its own limits per hour as well. The notice used to be outside these limits, so this changes what is spent: nothing in your settings is rewritten, and the stricter of the two limits is the one that stops a call. If the notice needs more than your daily total allows, raise the total.
- **The summary by volume counts only what a channel sends, survives a restart and reaches the channels that asked for what it holds**: events that no channel would send no longer count for the threshold, what is held is kept in the state file, and a summary of stuck builds goes to a channel that asked only for stuck builds.
- **A failure nobody fixes keeps weighing on the score, and open collaboration alerts count with their age**: a workflow that stayed red lowered the recovery part for seven days and then the part went back to 100 when the failing run left the window. Now the stretch of the red that falls inside the window is measured (the window before counted its own, so no day is counted twice) and the ledger keeps the last failure of a workflow nobody fixed for as long as the rule of the red shows it. An alert of the collaboration sentinel that is still open at the end of the window counts with the time it has been open so far, so leaving alerts unresolved lowers the collaboration part instead of leaving it without data. Scores can be lower than before after the update; the Score guide explains it.
- **A chained deployment that fails on the default branch is red**: runs of the events workflow_run and repository_dispatch now count in the red of a repository, but only on the default branch and never when they come from a fork. A deployment chained to the CI that failed on main used to appear in no list at all. The setting that names the events (ci.red_events) still decides, and its default and its help in eight languages say it.
- **The frozen contract of the read API goes down to the elements of the lists**: `/api/data` was frozen as `data: any`, so renaming the list of runs or dropping the repositories or the allowance kept `CONTRACT=PASS`; the contract now describes the runs, the repositories, the allowance and the figures of `/api/data`, the elements of the alerts of `/api/alerts` (a list can be written as `[{...}]` and its first three elements are checked) and `/api/auth/me`, which was a readable route outside both lists. The types of the fields of an alert are frozen too, not only their names. `docs/API.md` and its translations say so.

### Fixed

- **The shared files of the monitor are saved again when two processes write at the same time on Windows**: the temporary file has a name of its own and a refused replace is tried again, so the count of the AI quota is no longer lost.
- **The wall screens follow the language chosen on the main page**: a screen opened without `?lang=` came out in the language of the monitor (English) even when the person had chosen another one on the main page. The order is now `?lang=` of the address, then the language saved by the main page in the same browser (a screen with another language reloads once, before it draws, keeping `?rotate=` and the other parameters), then the language of the monitor; the link "Open screen" of Settings carries `?lang=` of the page. It holds in the eight languages, with the font of Japanese and Chinese and the locale of each language for the time, the date and the numbers. Tests check the three screens in the eight languages with the preference saved, that `?lang=` wins and that no English text is left; the guide of the screens explains how the language is decided.
- **The integrated AI no longer hangs when two requests arrive at once**: two explanations at the same time, or an explanation while the budget page is read, took the two locks of the chain in opposite orders and waited for each other until the monitor was restarted. The locks are now always taken in the same order, and the check of the budget of a model and the count of its call are one step, so requests at once cannot jump the limit of a model.
- **A late delivery of the webhook no longer turns a red run green or a green one red**: the delivery replaced the run the cycle had already read with one whose kind of event was "webhook", which the rule of the red does not count, so the colour flipped for one cycle and the channels announced a false fixed and a false new red. The run now keeps the kind of event that GitHub sends, a delivery of a run already finished with the same result changes nothing, and one that changes its state adds to the run instead of replacing it.
- **The log of the failed step is one line, is kept for a short time and does not repeat a failure**: a step name with a line break could start a line of the brief that looked like an alert; terminal links and titles are removed from the log; an answer that could not read the log is kept five minutes (not for ever), a failure one minute, and the statistics of a workflow keep at most 200 names of step.
- **The summary by volume is told as soon as the last hour has room, and with the level of the worst thing it holds**: a new red held back could wait up to a day, and the summary always had the level warn, so a person who asked only for the serious ones never received it.
- **The Live view names the workflow of the last event**: the last event always showed an empty name.
- **Several processes can share the store of the AI without passing its limits**: the store is now read, changed and written under a lock of the operating system (Windows and Linux), which the system itself releases when a process dies, so two processes can no longer each make their own quota; the existing rescue of lock files is untouched.
- **The key of a group of failed jobs is cut and cleaned**: when a job has no platform or parenthesis, the whole name of the job was the key of its group, without a limit and without cleaning; it is now one line of 80 characters at most, cleaned like the rest.
- **The branch, the fork mark, the last push and the pull requests of a repository reach every repository, not only the first one**: once the runs were in memory (from the first cycle on) a variable reused in the table of the Repositories columns made every repository but the first one lose what the sentinel knew of it. The forks without a CI of their own were not recognised as such, so their inherited crons entered the score and the figure of idle forks said 1; and a red CI on a default branch that is not main or master could miss the list of open failures and the health of the team. The score now leaves out the whole set of idle forks (not only the 200 that the page shows).
- **The health of the team and of the fleet counts every run of the cycle, not only the 600 newest of the account**: with 250 active repositories 215 of them said "no data" because their runs did not fit in the cut that the page receives. The runs, the failures of the last 24 hours and the runs of the week are now counted per repository before the cut, so a busy account shows its healthy repositories as healthy.
- **A red whose workflow is still being run no longer ages into "inactive"**: a failure older than the age (30 days by default) was hidden even when later runs of the same workflow had been cancelled, were in progress or were waiting for an approval. Now only a workflow that has not been run at all since the failure ages, and a workflow whose natural period is longer than the age (a monthly schedule that always fails) waits for twice its usual interval before it does.
- **Leftovers of the storage on Windows and on damaged files**: the state of the reports is no longer written back after a read that failed (it dropped the other accounts and their memory); a document that exists and cannot be understood (a damaged byte, a cut file) is kept as a .damaged copy before a new one is written, instead of being erased; a lock whose owner is still alive is not robbed after a short time and a refused delete no longer makes the process that waits spin without looking at its deadline; and the log of actions and the audit of accesses still record an action when the rotation is refused because a reader has the file open (the failed rotations are counted).
- **The packaging gate proves that the zip is the commit**: besides comparing the names of the files with the versioned ones, it now fails when a versioned file has been edited and not committed (it would travel with the edit), when two names differ only in capitals (one would overwrite the other on Windows and macOS), and when git does not answer in the folder, instead of skipping the comparison without a word (pass --allow-no-git to build from an extracted zip; the gate then says that the comparison was not made).
- **Four small rules now do what their text says**: the share of the request budget kept for repositories with a red CI belongs to the hour and is no longer granted again at every cycle (a budget of 100 per hour with twelve red repositories spent about 880); a run that waits for a runner of your own that is off stays a breakdown instead of turning into a ghost after seven days; a list read in pages keeps the pages already read when a later page fails and says that it is cut (the jobs of a run of 240 jobs showed none); and a workflow that ran after its permissions were read is no longer kept apart as switched off by hand.
- **An unfixed failure keeps counting in the success rate, and the batched read keeps what it does not name**: the score no longer rises just because one more day passes with a workflow in red (its failing run left the window but the failure did not), and the optional read by batches (network.graphql_checks) no longer erases a red workflow that did not run on the last commit (a cron, a workflow with a path filter).
- **`lint_workflows.py --remote` no longer reports a repository it could not read as clean**: a repository that does not exist (or that the token cannot see), an organization whose SSO is not authorised, no network, or a name that is not `OWNER/REPO` ended with `0 finding(s)` and exit code 0, so a pre-push step written with it passed without having looked at a single workflow. The exit code is now 2 in those cases (a finding still gives 1), the reason is printed on the standard error (a repository that is not there is told apart from one that simply has no workflows) and `.` and `..` are no longer accepted as an owner or repository name. `--json` keeps its shape. `docs/CONSOLE_TOOLS.md` and its translations say so.
- **A red that ages out and an inactive workflow are no longer invisible**: when a failing workflow had not run for `ci.inactive_days`, or its failure was older than `ci.red_age_days`, it left the list of reds and nothing on the page said so, although the help of both settings promises that it is shown as inactive. The Now tab now has a line with how many failed workflows no longer count as red and another with the dormant and inactive repositories, in the eight languages.
- **`--demo` now holds the frozen contract and writes nothing into its folder**: `python bin/actions_monitor.py --demo` left a key of the contract out of the answer about discovery, the demo of `tools/` left two out of the answer about the update, and the score named no account, so `tools/check_api_contract.py` gave `CONTRACT=FAIL` against all of them and nobody could tell whether the failure was theirs. The demo also wrote `ai/LESSONS.md` (and the book of lessons) into the folder it ran from; with `--demo` nothing is written any more. A test now checks the contract against the demo in both editions.
- **Four guides said things the code does not do**: the webhook checklist asked to re-send the test event from GitHub and look for it in `?view=live` (a re-sent delivery is ignored as a repeat and a `ping` names no repository, so it is never listed: the step now says to run a workflow); the wall screens guide said that with `screens.enabled` off every address answers 404 (`/screen/1` to `/screen/3` are the page itself and keep working, and the lite edition serves the wall screens too); the lite guide listed six tabs for the complete edition (it has ten) and said that the AI paths answer 404 (the channels of the feed for an AI tool answer in both editions); and the plain-text README and two launchers asked for Python 3.10 when the monitor needs 3.11 (the Japanese message was missing too). Tests read the tab list, the lite paths and the launchers.
- **The shell launchers keep their executable bit in the repository, and the zip no longer depends on the machine that builds it**: the six `.sh` and `.command` launchers were versioned without it, so a clone on Linux or macOS answered "Permission denied" to the command the README gives. The zip carried the line endings of the builder's checkout (Windows gave CRLF in every text file, Linux LF, so the same commit gave two different zips); text files now travel with LF and the Windows launchers with CRLF.
