- **The command line and the About window: five small faults**: `credential reload` and `credential replace` were always refused (403) by a monitor with `session_guard` on, which is the default, because the command never asked for the one-use token the page asks for (it does now); a token piped in with a BOM (what PowerShell adds) was read wrongly; the warning about plain http to another computer came after the password had been sent (it comes first now); with `--ca-file` an address of this computer went through the proxy of the environment; and a refused HTTPS certificate was reported as "cannot be reached" (it now says the certificate is not trusted and points to `--ca-file`). The version shown in the About window is escaped like every other text.
- **The test count in the README is the real count of test methods, a guide stops naming a function as a setting, and the browser no longer gets a 404 for its icon**: the README counted only the test classes whose base is called TestCase and left out the ones that inherit from a base of their own (2912 against 3314 methods; a run prints a higher number still because each subclass runs the methods it inherits); `docs/LESSONS.md` named the import of a book of failures as if it were a setting; and `/favicon.ico`, the only 404 of the page and of the wall screens, now answers 204. Tests sweep every setting named in the guides and the secret of the webhook in the file.
- **With several accounts and the team mode, a route under `/api/v1/` needs the same role as the same route without the prefix**: the supervisor judged the path with the prefix, so a viewer could read through the versioned API what only an administrator reads (the exposure of the monitor, the state of the token, the log of actions). The prefix is now taken off before the roles are asked, and the answer keeps its `X-API-Version` header.
- **A repository whose visibility was not read is private on the wall screens**: with a list of repositories written by hand, or an older `gh`, every repository counted as public and `screens.show_repo_names` showed the names of private ones. The visibility is now read with one listing of the account every `discover_refresh_seconds`, and what could not be read is shown as private.
- **A captured webhook delivery cannot be renewed by changing its delivery id, and garbage cannot use up the allowance of the real deliveries**: the signed body is remembered as well as the id, and the requests whose signature does not verify are counted apart (`webhook.max_bad_per_minute`, 120 a minute and address, in every mode and by the address behind a trusted proxy) from the ones that do (120 a minute).
- **A process of an account only holds the webhook secret its own configuration names**: the secrets of the other accounts and of the main configuration stayed in its environment and in the one of `gh` and of every program it started.
- **The variable of a secret can no longer be a variable of the system or of the login to GitHub**: `webhook.secret_env` and `listen.token_env` refuse names like PATH or GH_TOKEN, and the name of the webhook secret is read once, when the process starts.
- **With several accounts the supervisor protects every answer against framing**, like the process of an account does.
- **A cursor with a letter that is not ASCII is a plain 400, and any other failure of the versioned API is the error envelope of a 500 (`api.server_error`)** instead of a cut connection with a trace in the console.
- **The versioned API asks for the access token before it says whether it is on, and a cursor belongs to who asked for it**: a visitor without credentials got a 404 from `/api/v1/` when the switch was off and a 401 when it was on, and the same cursor was valid for any person and account. Now the token comes first, and the cursor of one person or account is refused to another.
- **The supervisor tells the process of each account, signed, who acts and with which role**: with several accounts the roles were not known to the account, so the log of actions ignored `audit_log.maintainers_can_read` and `GET /api/v1/me` said the team mode was off. The context is signed with the key of that process, is good for 30 seconds and for that account only, and without it the name of a user alone is not believed.
- **Webhook deliveries are remembered after a restart, old events can be refused, and the allowance of good requests is a setting**: the memory is a bounded private file (`webhook.persist_deliveries`, on by default), `webhook.reject_stale` (off by default) answers and ignores a signed workflow event older than `webhook.replay_seconds`, and `webhook.max_verified_per_minute` (120) is the allowance of correctly signed requests of one address.
- **Every setting that names a variable with a secret names only its own**: the key of the AI, the channels of notices, the webhook and the access token refuse the variables of the system and of the login to GitHub and the secrets that belong to another setting, and a name can be written in one setting only.
- **The numbers of the hidden names on the wall screens follow an HMAC of the name**: with a key of the installation kept in a private file of the data folder (never in the configuration), only the hidden repositories are numbered, and the number says nothing about where a repository falls among the others.
- **The log of actions can be made a condition of the actions**: with `audit_log.required` on (off by default) an action that has to be recorded is refused with a 503 while the log cannot be written; the settings stay open so that it can be put right.
- **The log of actions keeps a number of rotated files that you choose**: `audit_log.keep_generations` (5 by default, 1 to 20) keeps the old parts as `.1`, `.2` and so on, and the chain is checked across all of them.
- **The forks that GitHub does not give back no longer hold the follow-up of the original repositories**: a fork that came back empty (deleted, renamed or not visible), or a request that failed, was the first of every turn for ever. They are written down as tried, the panel counts them as unreadable, and the turn takes the next ones.
- **The batch way of the lookup of hidden workflows reads the listing of a repository once**: when the listing did not bring a workflow it was asked again in every turn and nothing was ever resolved. The listing is read once per repository inside the time the answers are kept, and what it did not bring goes one by one in the same turn.
- **A question of the sentinel that failed waits before it is asked again**: the hidden workflows, the jobs of a red run and the permissions of a repository are asked again after 5 minutes, then 30, then 6 hours, and a repository whose permissions cannot be read goes to the end of the line, so it no longer keeps the other repositories from being read.
- **The hourly budget of run lists (`sampling.api_budget_per_hour`) counts requests**: the second look at the default branch (`sampling.default_branch_probe`) is counted when the repositories of a cycle are chosen, and the duration of a re-run (`ci.rerun_timing`) is one request of the hour that is not made when the budget is spent.
- **The webhook guide says what the code does**: the repository is read again in the next cycle after an event, the names of the workflow, branch and job are kept as third-party text, and the live events can be read by whoever may open the page.
- **No route that answers a GET reads GitHub any more**: the runners, the organisations of the account, the update notice and the allowance of every source are read by the cycle, and the routes show what was read (`/api/runners` and the status of the quota say they are pending until the first reading).
- **The duration of a re-run has an allowance for each reading of each repository**: the count of three requests was shared by the threads of a cycle.
- **The follow-up of the originals of forks keeps the requests of the hour across a restart and says how long one pass takes**: when it is longer than the time between readings the panel says that the follow-up never rests.
- **The folders that an earlier run left with the addresses of the Apprise services are removed when the monitor starts**, not at the first send, which could be hours away.
- **The "bing bing" no longer stays silent for most of what happens**: it only looked at the red workflows, so alerts of the other sentinels, reports and lessons never sounded; a sound that the browser blocked was lost for good because the alert was already counted as seen; one pause of thirty seconds for everything swallowed a second kind of event that came a moment after the first; and a window of the wall created a new audio context for every beep, which a browser keeps blocked until someone clicks. Now every alert of the list, every new finding of the reports and every new lesson is watched; what the browser blocks waits (for at most ten minutes) and plays at the first click, when the page comes back to the front or at the next check every two seconds; each kind has its own pause and one burst of at most three sounds, the most serious first, keeps them from piling up; and the wall screens keep one audio context and a small queue.
- **The incoming webhook counts its rate limit against the visitor, not against the reverse proxy**: the limit of 120 requests a minute was counted against the address of the connection, which behind a proxy is always the proxy, and before the signature was checked, so one visitor sending unsigned requests made the signed deliveries of everybody else answer 429. It is now counted against the address that a trusted proxy adds to the forwarding header (the connection itself when no proxy is trusted).
- **Taking a proxy variable out of the environment of a container switches the setting off again**: an empty `MONITOR_TRUSTED_PROXY`, `MONITOR_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, `MONITOR_SECURE_COOKIE`, `MONITOR_PROXY_PUBLIC_HOST` or `MONITOR_ALLOWED_HOSTS` left the value of an earlier start in the configuration file, so a proxy that was no longer declared stayed trusted. The empty variable now clears its key.
- **A request that carries no access token is refused but no longer counts as a wrong guess**: ten page loads without a token locked the address of the visitor for five minutes. Only a token that was presented and is wrong counts now.
- **The guide and the Compose file give the login file to the user of the container**: a file of mode 600 that belongs to the person who made it cannot be read by the user 10001 of the container, so the account stayed blind with the reason missing. The command now also gives the file to that user.
- **The health answer of the supervisor has the keys of the frozen contract, and the contract marks the route that only the computer of the monitor may use**: `/api/health` in the container lacked the version, the uptime, the cycle and the credential, and the contract tool failed against a container. The tool now accepts the refusal of a route marked as local only when it asks from the network.
- **The calls of a tick of the Sentinel are one count for all its stages, and a repository whose reads keep failing waits**: the limit of a tick was applied to each stage by itself (150 calls in a tick of 70), and a repository the server answered with an error was read again in every cycle. Now the stages share the count and such a repository is left alone for 30 seconds, doubling up to 10 minutes.

## [2.0.8]

### Security

- **The shared secret of the incoming webhook only travels in the environment (`webhook.secret_env`, `MONITOR_WEBHOOK_SECRET` by default)**: it was a text in `actions_monitor.json`, where a backup, a copied configuration or a shared screen could leak it. The receiver now reads it from the environment variable named by `webhook.secret_env` (or from the file named by that variable plus `_FILE`, the way a container hands over a secret), the monitor takes it out of its own environment once it has read it so the programs it starts do not inherit it, and a secret written in the file is ignored. The old way can be kept for a migration with `webhook.allow_config_secret` (file only, off by default) and the Live view warns while it is used. The Settings tab shows the name of the variable and whether the secret is defined, never the secret.

### Added

- **The webhook also takes `check_run` events, and the Live view says how much of the time the events cover the state**: `check_run` joins the closed list of events (`workflow_run`, `workflow_job`, `check_run`, `push`, `release`, `check_suite` and `ping`); like the others it only marks the repository for a read and keeps no text. The Live view and `/api/live-status` (new `coverage` and `secret_source`) say whether the state is covered by events (the last one is newer than `savings.webhook_window_minutes`), has had no event for a while (a quiet repository or a lost tunnel) or waits for the first one. The polling stays the fallback, so a delivery that never arrived is caught by the next read.
- **A guide to set the webhook up in GitHub, in eight languages**: `docs/WEBHOOK.md` lists, step by step, how to make the secret, how to give it to the monitor in PowerShell, a shell, a service or a container, what to fill in on the webhook page of GitHub (address, content type, secret, events) and what each answer (`404`, `401`, `413`, `400`, `202` and `429`) means.

### Changed

- **The paid runners of another company that the model recognises by default include `codspeed`** (`runners.model.thirdparty_labels`): public repositories use it for their benchmarks, and its jobs were counted as a runner nobody knew. A list you wrote yourself is not touched.

### Fixed

- **The documents no longer say that macOS runs in the CI**: the README and its seven translations said that the tests run in the CI on three systems and that Linux and macOS are tested in the CI; since the CI tests on Ubuntu and Windows only (macOS will be taken up again later), they now say so.
- **A workflow whose file was deleted is not one of the workflows you can enable**: GitHub keeps the history of a deleted workflow in the list with an empty path (six of the 96 public repositories studied have one), and the hygiene check offered it as a workflow disabled by hand. It now stays out of the lists that need a file.
- **A fork that was never pushed is not "recently pushed"**: a fork inherits the push date of its upstream when it is made (693 of the 722 forks of an account measured have it before their own creation), so the sentinel took every new fork for the most active repository of the account and read it as a hot one. The push date of a fork is now ignored while it is not newer than its creation.

## [2.0.7]

### Added

- **A read API under `/api/v1/` for clients (setting `api.v1_enabled`, off by default)**: every read route is also served under the prefix (the same answer and roles, plus the header `X-API-Version: 1`), and three routes are new: `/api/v1/me` (who is asking: the mode, the user, the role and the accounts of the team session), `/api/v1/repos` (the watched repositories, one line each, in pages with a signed cursor, `fields=` and `profile=compact`, gzip when the client accepts it) and `/api/v1/openapi.json`. The new routes answer errors in one envelope (`error`, a stable `code` such as `api.bad_cursor` and a `message` in the language asked). Everything is read only, from what the monitor already holds. The shape is frozen in the new section `v1_endpoints` of the contract (`python tools/check_api_contract.py --v1`); the limits are the settings `api.default_limit`, `api.max_limit`, `api.cursor_ttl_seconds` and `api.gzip_min_bytes`. Texts in eight languages, the guide of the API updated in all of them and a test with the real server.
- **A workflow the owner switched off by hand is no longer a red (setting `ci.disabled_apart`, on by default)**: a fork copies the nightly job of the project it follows, the job fails for lack of the secrets, the owner switches the workflow off, and its last run stayed "failure" for ever (two accounts of the study showed it). Now a workflow in the state `disabled_manually` leaves the list of reds and is counted apart in the panel ("switched off by hand"). A workflow GitHub switched off for inactivity is not touched, because that is not a decision of the owner. Text in eight languages and a test.
- **The original repository of each fork can be followed (setting `upstream.enabled`, off by default)**: for an account that collects forks the useful question is what happens in the projects it follows, not whether its own CI is green. With the switch on, the monitor reads the original of each fork (the status of the checks of the last commit of its default branch and its latest release) and shows them in the hygiene panel of the Sentinel; with `upstream.notify` it also gives an information notice when the original is red or published a release. The cost is bounded by settings in the configuration file: `upstream.requests_per_hour` (6), `upstream.forks_per_request` (8), `upstream.refresh_hours` (12), `upstream.news_days` (7) and `upstream.repos` (the forks to follow; empty means all). One request reads several forks, the least recently read go first, and nothing is written anywhere. Texts in eight languages and a test with the cases of the study.

## [2.0.6]

### Added

- **The three wall screens are drawn**: Overview (score ring and letter, fleet lamps, 24-hour and 7-day area charts, a heat map by repository and hour, what has been red the longest, runners and minutes, the latest events and the allowance as a gauge), Right now (running runs with progress, the queue, runs waiting for approval, recent failures, merges and releases, and a live line of the last two hours) and Sentinels (one card for each sentinel with its level, open findings, readable or not and the last sweep, an animated radar and the alerts by severity). The page draws every chart itself (no library), counters and rings animate, a new failure flashes once and old ones calm down (`screens.blink_calm_minutes`), nothing moves for who asked for less motion, a monitor that does not answer dims the page and is tried again after longer and longer waits, everything shifts a few pixels every minute against burn-in (`screens.burn_in_protection`), the page can dim at night (`screens.night_dim`) and one window can turn through the three pages with `?rotate=30`. The Settings tab opens them with a link, and a browser test checks the three pages in 1080p, 4K and turned, in the eight languages, with and without less motion.

## [2.0.5]

### Added

- **Forks enter the hygiene, the lint of workflows and the score only when they have a CI of their own (settings `ci.forks_need_own_ci` and `ci.fork_own_ci_days`, default 90 days)**: a fork copies the files, the rulesets, the releases and the workflows of a project somebody else owns, so judging its hygiene (no ruleset, no release, the licence of the upstream) or its workflow files is judging the upstream, and an account that collects forks (722 of 727 repositories in the one measured, none with a ruleset, a protection or a release) showed hundreds of warnings and a score made of noise. A fork now counts when it ran a push, pull request, manual or merge queue run of its own in the window; a schedule it inherited does not count, nor does the pull request of another fork, nor the runs GitHub makes for itself. When the list of its runs is full and does not reach back to the start of the window the fork stays in, because a run of its own may hide further back. The forks left out are counted apart (`a6.forks_idle` of the sentinel, the header figure `forks_idle`, a chip in the hygiene panel), their runs are not part of the score, and with the switch off every fork is judged as before.
- **Dormant and inactive repositories are a state of their own, neither green nor red (setting `ci.dormant_repos`)**: an account that collects forks has hundreds of repositories that have never run anything (724 of 727 in the account measured), and a repository whose newest run is months old is not healthy either. Each watched repository is now active, dormant (it has never run anything of its own, its list of runs is complete and empty), inactive (its newest run is older than `ci.inactive_days`) or unknown (the read failed or the list is cut). They are counted apart in the figures of the header (`dormant`, `inactive_repos`), in the data of the monitor and in the snapshot of the score (`activity`), and the tooltip of the score chip says how many there are. The score is calculated only from what runs. The workflows GitHub makes for itself are not runs of the repository.
- **Runs queued for days are told apart as a ghost queue (setting `ci.stuck.ghost_enabled`, days in `ci.stuck.ghost_days`, default 7)**: on public repositories GitHub leaves runs in the queue for weeks or months (a closed pull request, a job that asks for a runner that does not exist; 280 days at the worst, in 8 of 10 busy repositories). Those runs inflated the queued count, were reported as stuck builds and fed every limit learned from the queue. A queued run of the first attempt older than the limit is now neither queued nor stuck: it leaves the list "Right now" and the counts, a line says how many there are, and the sentinel lists them apart and gives one information notice per repository (`ci.ghost_queue`), never an error. The switch is on by default; turned off, every queued run is treated as before.

### Changed

- **A red nobody attends to ages after 30 days and becomes inactive (settings `ci.red_ages` and `ci.red_age_days`)**: a fork copies the nightly job of the project it follows, the job fails every night for lack of the secrets, the owner switches the workflow off, and the last run stays "failure" for ever, so the repository was red for ever (41 of 42 runs and 26 of 27 in the two cases measured, the last one nearly a year old). Until now only a workflow that had not run at all for `ci.inactive_days` (90) days stopped being red, and a workflow that went on running pull requests never did. Now a failing run older than `ci.red_age_days` days (30 by default) with nothing newer of the events that decide leaves the red list and is listed as inactive; the rows say it aged. The switch is on by default; turned off, a red stays red until the workflow runs green again.
- **The hygiene check of the default branch judges the rules in force by default (`sentinel.hygiene.ruleset_mode` is now `effective`)**: before, the branch counted as protected only when a ruleset with an exact name (`protect-main`) was active, which no studied account used (none of the studied accounts named its rulesets like that, and 32 of 96 repositories protect the branch in the classic way). By default a branch now counts as protected when GitHub reports a rule or a protection for it, whatever it is called. The check reads the branch and its rules, two more requests for each repository every time the hygiene is renewed. Set `name` to bring back the old rule. A profile that asks for the old rule keeps it.

### Fixed

- **The protection of the default branch is judged from every public source, not only from the rules of the rulesets**: 32 of the 96 public repositories studied have a default branch protected in the classic way, which `rules/branches` does not show, so an empty list of rules was read as "no protection". In `effective` mode the answer of the branch now also gives its classic `protection` (whether it is enabled and how strictly its status checks apply, readable without admin rights) and the three sources are joined; a branch is unprotected only when they answered and none of them protects it, and an answer that did not come (a timeout, a refused read, a missing field) stays "not evaluable". Each row of the hygiene panel says what protects the branch (`protection`: ruleset, classic, both or none).
- **The workflows GitHub makes for itself are neither red CI nor files**: the entries with a path that starts with `dynamic/` (Dependabot updates, the dependency graph, the default CodeQL, the review and the agent of Copilot) failed for ever in 20 of the 96 public repositories studied, and were counted as workflows of the repository (red, abandoned, "disabled"). The monitor kept only the file name of a run, so the check of the path never matched; now the whole path is remembered when the run is read. Those workflows never decide the colour of a branch, are not counted as workflows to check, and are not listed among the workflows disabled by hand or by inactivity. A failing Dependabot update is listed apart as a warning, and the others (Copilot, CodeQL, the dependency graph) as information, in `a2.services` of the sentinel and in the header figure `services_failing`.
- **The lookup of a hidden workflow (A2) obeys the same origin rule as the rest of the red**: when the sample of runs is full, the last run of a workflow is asked for on its own, and that answer used to take any run of the branch, also the one of a pull request of a fork, an issue or a comment (the `exclude_pull_requests` filter of GitHub does not remove them because a fork's run has no pull request attached). Now the answer keeps only runs of an event in `ci.red_events` that do not come from a fork, the runs GitHub makes for itself are not taken for workflows of the repository, and the event is kept with the answer. Tests use the shapes measured on seven public repositories.
- **Questions are asked inside the page, so they show in full screen**: the five confirmations of the page (change of mode, run a command proposed by the AI, a GitHub action, a local workflow run, delete a user) used the dialog of the browser, which is not drawn when the page is in full screen (the person had to press Esc to see it) and blocks the page. They use one dialog of the page (`askConfirm`) with Accept and Cancel, Esc and Tab, the focus on Cancel for a dangerous action and back where it was afterwards. A test fails when a native `confirm`, `alert` or `prompt` comes back, and a test in a browser asks and answers with the mouse and the keyboard.

## [2.0.4]

### Added

- **A model for each task, a chain of fallbacks and a budget for each model, for the AI of the monitor** (all of it off until written in the settings, and only among the providers you name): `ai.tasks.build.model` (and the same for `codeql`, `report`, `diagnose` and `alert`, each with its own provider and its own limits) lets another model answer that kind of question; `ai.fallback` lists the models tried in order when the main one fails (any error, a refusal of the key, a model that does not exist, a quota error, or a slow answer when `ai.fallback_after_seconds` is set) or has left the catalog, and `ai.fallback_max_attempts` caps the whole chain; the calls a day for each model (`ai.model_daily_limit`) and the estimated cost for each model and in total (`ai.model_cost_limit_daily`, `ai.cost_limit_daily`, only when the prices are known) are budgets on top of `ai.daily_limit`, and a model whose budget is spent is skipped, never jumped over. The first failure of the main model is written to the log and to the brief with its reason. With nothing set, the one general model answers as before.
- **Alerts, Reports and Lessons are three entries of the menu on the left**: the optional reports and the lessons from the failures were sections at the bottom of the Alerts tab; each now has its own entry with its own icon, badge (reports: repositories with findings and releases that are blocked; lessons: those that apply now) and address (`#reports`, `#lessons`, also `?view=reports` and `?view=lessons`). Nothing was lost or repeated, the old addresses (`#alerts`, `#alerts/reports`, `#al/lessons`) open the right panel, a panel of reports with none turned on says so instead of vanishing, and the screens can list the new tabs in `ui.screen1_tabs` (`rep`, `les`). The guides name the new menu entries in the eight languages.

### Fixed

- **"Help of this tab" opens the topic of the tab that is open in every tab**: the map from tab to topic was missing the Alerts and Team tabs and sent Repositories and History to the first topic of the Help ("GitHub Actions Monitor"). It is now one table (`TAB_TOPICS`) with an entry for every tab of the side menu, and a test fails when a tab has no topic or one that does not exist. A test in a browser goes through every tab and presses the button.
- **The History tab no longer piles up its count**: "Showing 200 of N" was appended to the tools of the table at every refresh while the tools themselves were drawn again only when the order or the columns changed, so thousands of copies grew next to the list of columns. The count is now one element that is replaced or removed. A test opens the page in a browser, draws the table many times (refresh, filters, all columns, an order) and checks that there is one count and one hint.

## [2.0.3]

### Security

- **Every link the page builds from data goes through the address filter**: the links to the last run, the repository, the board, the issue, the unresolved failure and the update notice (12 of 14 did not) only open an `https` address of GitHub or of the configured host; anything else becomes an inert `#`. A test fails if a new link is built without the filter.

### Added

- **The wall screens are off by default**: `screens.enabled` is now off until it is switched on in Settings, so `/screen/overview`, `/screen/now` and `/screen/sentinels` answer 404 on a new installation.
- **Wall screens: the addresses, the settings and the data** (the three pages are `/screen/overview`, `/screen/now` and `/screen/sentinels`, for a monitor or a TV that nobody touches): they only read what the monitor already holds through `/api/screens/data` (a closed list of fields: never GitHub, never a link, a commit title, a job name or a user name), they are off with `screens.enabled` (every address then answers 404), the names of private repositories follow `screens.show_repo_names` (hidden by default), failures blink less than twice a second and calm down after `screens.blink_calm_minutes` (`screens.blink`), and a window can only be made quieter by its address (`?blink=off`; `?sound=on` only when `screens.sound` allows it) or set with `?monitor`, `?theme`, `?density`, `?refresh`, `?lang`, `?scale` and `?rotate`. Also `screens.default_theme`, `screens.default_density`, `screens.refresh_seconds`, `screens.rotate_seconds`, `screens.burn_in_protection` and `screens.night_dim` (docs/SCREENS.md).
- **The skills for the AIs name no assistant, say how to connect by kind of tool and carry the version of the monitor**: the instructions no longer mention any company or assistant; a new reference (`references/connect.md`, and a table in the guide *Connect an AI tool* in the eight languages) says which way fits which tool (files, HTTP, MCP, stream, command line), how to install the skill and how its version follows the monitor, and what to do when an AI of the monitor does not answer; `GET /api/skills` now answers `monitor_version` and a `version` for each skill.
- **The models of the AI provider are read, checked and measured instead of typed blind** (the list is off for background use, read only, and no model name is built in): Settings > External AI > *Models and data per model* loads the list of the provider (`/models`, the equivalent of the Anthropic API, `/api/tags` of Ollama) with one read-only request, with a search and a button to use a model; the name is checked (exists, missing with similar names, or could not be checked) and, with `ai.catalog.enabled` on, a model that no longer appears (retired or renamed) is warned about; `ai.catalog.ttl_minutes`, `ai.catalog.max_requests_per_day` and `ai.catalog.fit_context` bound it. A table keeps the context window, the price per million tokens and the tools of each model, filled only with what the provider returns (empty = unknown, never a made up figure) and editable by hand. *Test this model* asks it about a synthetic failure and shows latency, tokens, estimated cost and a sample answer; `monitor_cli.py ai models`, `ai test` and `ai doctor` do the same from the terminal, and the doctor says why an AI does not answer (key variable not set, unknown model, quota, network) without printing a key. New routes: `GET /api/ai/models`, `POST /api/ai/models/refresh`, `POST /api/ai/models/test`, `POST /api/ai/models/data`, `POST /api/ai/doctor` (the posts from this computer only).
- **Reds with one root cause are told once, and a red run says which of its jobs failed** (both off by default): `ci.group.reusable` recognises the red workflows that call the same reusable workflow, shows one shared-cause line in the red list and sends one notification with the number of workflows and repositories affected instead of one each (no red row is hidden); `ci.failed_jobs` reads the jobs of a few red runs per cycle (one call each) and the red row names the failed jobs grouped by platform, so an umbrella workflow with a matrix of systems tells which ones fail.
- **A long build is not "stuck" just because it is long, and a stuck run says why it waits**: the block `ci.stuck` can learn the limit of each workflow from its own recent runs (`ci.stuck.learned`: a percentile of their durations times a margin, kept between a minimum and a maximum), fix a limit per workflow file or per runner label (`ci.stuck.workflow_minutes`, `ci.stuck.runner_minutes`) and give the runs that wait for a runner their own longer limit (`ci.stuck.queue_minutes`). Every stuck run now says why: it waits for a runner, for an approval, its runner is offline, or it is a build that runs long. All of it is off or empty by default, so the single `stuck_minutes` limit keeps working exactly as before.
- **Less noise when there are many alerts, or the same ones again and again** (block `alerts`, all off or empty by default): permanent silences by pattern `repository:workflow:branch` with a reason and an optional review date (`alerts.silences`; the Alerts tab shows them as silenced and one reminder is announced when the review date arrives); a class for short crons and services that fail now and then, announced only after several failures in a row or a high failure rate, once and cleared once (`alerts.service`); one notification for the new reds of the same commit (`alerts.group`); and one summary every so many minutes once more notifications than a threshold were sent in the last hour (`alerts.digest`). The existing channels are unchanged, and the lists and the events feed still show everything.
- **The workflow linter and the surface checks work without cloning the repositories**: with `lint.source` set to `auto` the monitor reads the folder `.github/workflows` of every repository that has no local clone through the contents API of GitHub (one call for the list and one for each file that is new or changed; a file whose SHA did not change is never read again; `lint.api.max_calls_per_tick`, `lint.api.max_files_per_repo` and `lint.api.refresh_seconds` keep the cost down, and the reading pauses while the allowance is low), and with `sparse` it brings only `.github` with a sparse clone of depth 1 (`lint.sparse.max_repos_per_tick`, `lint.sparse.refresh_seconds`). The same text feeds A13 and the workflow checks of A10 (unpinned actions, write-all, pull_request_target, schedules) and the resolution of A2. The default stays `clone`, so nothing changes until you ask. The A13 panel and the brief now say what was read from clones, through the API or from a sparse clone, and list what could not be evaluated with its reason (SSO not authorised, missing scope, allowance spent, no clone, too many or too big files, waiting for its turn); the checks that need a full clone (stacks, dependabot.yml, manifest, CHANGELOG) are named as not evaluable instead of guessed.
- **`lint_workflows.py --remote OWNER/REPO`** reads the workflow files of a repository from GitHub through `gh`, with no clone, and says why when it cannot.
- **Help for organizations and big accounts**: `--doctor` now says why a read of an organization would come back empty instead of leaving a mute "no data" (the token is not authorised for the single sign-on of the organization, a scope or a permission is missing, the list of private repositories is cut short, the login was refused, the allowance is spent) and estimates the reads an hour the account will cost under the current settings against the allowance of the token, with a hook for other parts of the monitor to add their share. A new guide, `ORGANIZATIONS.md` (eight languages), gives the smallest token for a personal account and for an organization (classic and fine-grained), the steps to authorise it for SSO, how to watch an account you do not own with a token that has no scope, and the known limits.
- **Renovate counts like Dependabot, and the linter tells your own actions from third-party ones**: the pull requests of `renovate` are listed in A4 as dependency updates (`sentinel.dependency_bots` names the accounts), and a repository with a Renovate configuration is not reported as missing an update bot. `lint.own_owners` and `lint.unpinned_own` mark an unpinned action as own or third-party and can lower or hide the own ones; three optional rules, off by default, find a job without `timeout-minutes` (`lint.rules.no_timeout`), `secrets: inherit` (`lint.rules.secrets_inherit`) and an own action that follows `@main` (`lint.rules.own_action_main`).
- **Bots apart from people**: with `sentinel.bots_apart` on, pull requests of accounts that GitHub itself marks as bots (type Bot, not a guess from the name) are counted apart in A4 (`other_bots`) and are left out of the check of old pull requests that wait for a person; the runs now carry `actor_bot`. The default changes nothing.
- **Profiles**: Settings > Basic and the third step of the first-start guide offer three ready-made groups of settings (owner or team, open-source organisation, solo developer) that set the hygiene check, forks and archived repositories, the linter, the release file checks and the separate count of bots in one click; the values go through the same checked path as the Save button, nothing changes until one is chosen, and `GET /api/settings` lists them as `presets`. A recipe for large accounts is in the new guide `docs/LARGE_ACCOUNTS.md`.
- **Fleet view for hundreds of repositories**: the Team tab has a new section that groups the repositories (by ecosystem, by label, by state or not at all), orders them (by risk, name, last run or state) and draws them one page at a time, so an account of 250 repositories keeps the page light. Labels are patterns of repository names (`fleet.labels`, for example `team-*=backend`), which is also how organisations or teams are told apart; `fleet.saved_filters` keeps ready-made filters; `fleet.group_by`, `fleet.sort` and `fleet.page_size` set the defaults. It reads `GET /api/team/fleet` (read only, the same health as the team ranking); the ranking above it does not change.
- **Kinds of runner, their queue and cost, runaway workflows and self-hosted runners that are down**: with `runners.model.enabled` on, the labels (`runs-on`) of the jobs tell GitHub-hosted, larger, paid third-party and self-hosted runners apart (`runners.model.thirdparty_labels`, `runners.model.large_labels` and `runners.model.self_labels` make the taxonomy yours). Settings > Advanced shows the queue by kind, the minutes and cost by kind (a third-party minute is priced only if `runners.model.thirdparty_usd_per_minute` is set, and GitHub never bills it), the workflows that started at least `runners.model.runaway_runs_per_hour` times in the last hour, and the jobs that wait for a self-hosted runner for `runners.model.queue_warn_minutes` or more with the reason (no online runner has their labels, every fitting runner is busy, or an idle one does not take them). Both warnings are alerts (`runners.model.alerts`, level `runners.model.alert_level`, `warning` by default); it is read only, off by default and reads `runners.model.jobs_per_cycle` runs per cycle through `GET /api/runners/model`. With `finops.jobs_per_cycle` on, a job on a third-party runner is no longer priced as a GitHub-hosted one.
- **The inputs of a workflow fired by hand are listed before anything is fired**: `plan --inputs` of the batch dispatcher shows, under each target, the inputs its workflow asks for (name, type, required or optional, default, options of a choice and description), read from the workflow file at `origin/main` of the local clone. Nothing is fired or sent; `--json` adds an `inputs` list to every row (`null` when it could not be read). A new guide section lists the known limits: samples of runs, the plan that stays `unknown` for private and free repositories, one owner for each monitor and the merge queue.
- **Merge queue**: a failed run of the merge queue of the default branch (`gh-readonly-queue/...`, event `merge_group`) is a red of its own, shown as "(merge queue)" in the red list, the alerts and the team view, and it clears with the next green run of the queue.
- **Maintenance branches**: `ci.extra_branches` takes patterns (for example `release-*`) of extra branches whose last run also counts; by default only the default branch is watched. A red of such a branch is labelled with its name and never hides, or is hidden by, the default branch.
- **Honest coverage of the list of runs**: the Repositories table has a new column, *Sample reaches back*, that says how far back in time the list of runs of each repository goes. A full list that reaches back less than `sampling.min_coverage_minutes` (30 by default; 0 turns it off) is marked *low coverage* instead of looking complete, a list shorter than what was asked for is *complete*, and a total that GitHub stops counting (2,500 or 40,000) is shown as *at least*. A notice above the table counts the repositories with low coverage; the block `sampling` of `GET /api/data` carries the same numbers.
- **Second look at the default branch** (`sampling.default_branch_probe`, off by default): when the list of runs of a repository is full, one more request asks for the pushes to its default branch with pull requests left out, so the runs that decide red on main are not pushed out by pull requests and schedules. The runs that only the second look found never make a list count as full.
- **Budget of requests per hour** (`sampling.api_budget_per_hour`, 0 = none): past it the repositories with a red workflow are read first and the rest wait for the next hour, and the page says how many wait. The page also shows the requests per hour that the number of repositories and the intervals ask for (from 3,500 on, or whenever a budget is set), so a large account is warned before it runs out of allowance.
- **Long lists are read to the end, and a cut is said**: the workflows of a repository (148 or 348 are real cases), the jobs of a run (a matrix of more than 100) and the open issues and pull requests of a repository are now read page by page while the last page was full, up to `pagination.workflow_pages`, `pagination.job_pages` and `pagination.collab_pages` pages of 100. A repository that fits in one page costs the same requests as before. When something is still left out the page says "seen N of M": under the run for its jobs, in the hygiene of the repository for its workflows, and in the footer of the Team tab for repositories with more open items than were read and for the account when the Search API has more than its 1000 results.
- **A load test with a synthetic account**: 250 repositories and 1,250 workflows, no network, measures the requests of a cycle, the time of the cycle and of the team health, the memory of the held data and how many requests the lookups of A2 need, with wide bounds so that a loaded computer passes.

### Changed

- **The tests of the repository no longer run on macOS for now**: the macOS job was taken out of the CI workflow (every pull request, every push to main, every tag and the nightly run test on Ubuntu and Windows), and the workflow says that macOS support will be taken up again later. Nothing of the monitor changes.
- **The frozen shape of the API goes one level deeper**: the keys of the nested objects of the read answers (the cycle and the credential of the health, the loop of the data, the folders, the links, the update notice, the counters of the status bar and of the alerts), the exact `code` of the error of an unknown repository, the health of one repository and the envelope of the sentinel answers are part of the contract now. The check tool follows nested objects, and the test feeds a server with the invented data of the demo to check the answers that need data.
- **Forks and archived repositories are chosen by a setting, and a maintained fork is never lost**: `discovery.skip_archived` (on by default) decides whether archived repositories are watched; `discovery.fork_policy` can leave forks out (`skip`) or keep only the ones that have a CI of their own (`skip_unless_ci`: an enabled workflow and a run inside `discovery.fork_ci_days`), and `auto` does the same with a window twice as long on an organisation, whose kind is read once a day; `discovery.fork_checks_per_pass` limits the requests spent on it. The default (`show`) changes nothing.
- **The check of release files adapts to the project**: `sentinel.surface.checks.release_assets.require_sha256` and `.require_signature` stop asking for a checksum or a signature file, `.allow_empty` accepts a release without files (projects that publish to a package registry), `sentinel.surface.checks.tag_vs_version.prefixes` compares tags such as `cli-v1.2.0` with the manifest, and `.prerelease_ok` treats the betas of one day of a calendar version as the same version; `sentinel.require_verified_signature` was already switchable. The defaults ask for what they asked before.
- **A red on the default branch only counts when the repository itself caused it**: only runs of the events `push`, `schedule`, `workflow_dispatch` and `merge_group` decide (setting `ci.red_events`), and a run of a pull request that comes from a fork never does, even when the branch of the fork is also called `main`, `master` or `dev` (setting `ci.count_fork_runs`). The same rule feeds the red tile, the alerts, the team view, the brief and the green-without-release check.
- **A run that waits for an approval is its own state, not a red and not a queue**: `action_required` (a pull request of a fork or a first contribution that nobody approved) and a job held by the protection of an environment no longer turn a workflow red or inflate the queued count; the Now tab shows a separate line "waiting for approval" and the figures carry `awaiting`.
- **An abandoned workflow is "inactive", not red for ever**: a workflow whose last run failed but which has not run for `ci.inactive_days` days (90 by default, 0 turns it off) leaves the red list and is counted in the `inactive` figure.
- **Workflows that only react to issues, comments or labels do not count as red CI**: they are told apart by the events they run on (setting `ci.automation_counts`, off by default); every red row now says which kind of workflow it is.
- **The release gate checks the zip against the versioned files**: the zip must hold exactly the files the repository versions in the product folder (the example configuration, which the build makes, is the only exception); a versioned file that would not travel, whatever its suffix or folder, and a stray file that would travel without being versioned both stop the release. A short list of intended exclusions (`NOT_SHIPPED`) is empty. A test also covers the rule that every data file under `src/` travels.
- **Hygiene (A6) no longer assumes the conventions of one owner**: the name of the ruleset is a setting (`sentinel.hygiene.ruleset_names`, patterns allowed); `sentinel.hygiene.ruleset_mode` can judge the default branch by the rules GitHub reports as in force (public endpoints, no admin rights needed) whatever the ruleset is called; `sentinel.hygiene.skip` leaves a check out; `sentinel.hygiene.community_fallback` also counts the community profile and the `.github` repository of the account; a licence called LICENSE-MIT, LICENSE-APACHE, LICENSE.txt, COPYING or recognised by GitHub is accepted; and what the token cannot know (no admin rights) is shown as not evaluable, never green and never a warning. The defaults keep the old rule.
- **A2 settles many workflows with one request**: a repository with at least `sentinel.a2_resolve_batch_min` workflows to look up (2 by default; 0 = one by one) is asked once for its newest completed runs of the default branch, pull requests left out, and every workflow found in them is settled together (`sentinel.a2_resolve_batch_pages` pages of 100 at most); what the list does not show is asked one by one as before, so a repository with 100 or more workflows converges in a few cycles instead of four workflows per cycle.
- **The health of the team is fast with thousands of repositories**: the runs, red workflows, running runs and alerts are grouped by repository once, so a request to the team health no longer walks all of them for each repository (2,000 repositories took about eight seconds), and the health of one repository builds only its own view.

### Fixed

- **A group of the readiness chips says why it has no number, and a repository without any workflow no longer lowers its coverage**: a repository whose clone has no workflow file and which has no run is "not applicable": it is left out of the coverage of its group (so a group of five where two are new and empty is measured on the other three) and is listed apart in the tooltip and in the table. A group that is still below `sentinel.readiness.min_coverage` shows the words "not enough data" (not a bare "?") and its tooltip says how many repositories have data against the minimum and which ones lack it. The default minimum is not changed.
- **The account score says how long the calibration lasts and why something is missing**: while it calibrates the chip says how much is left of `score.calibrating_hours` with a bar and the day and hour the number will appear (`data_quality.calibrating_hours` is new), "not enough data" says which condition fails (runs or coverage), and `GET /api/score` carries `missing_detail` with the reason a part is not counted (its sentinel is off, it has not finished its first sweep and how many repositories it has read, it has read too few, its sources cannot be read, or the data is ready for the next pass).
- **The ring of the last 24 hours counts from one source and never shows a negative number**: the total in the centre and the three figures of the legend (successful, failed, cancelled or other) come from the server (`figures.done24` is new) and always add up; before, the total was counted on the list of runs the page receives, which holds only the newest ones, and "cancelled or other" went negative. A figure that is negative or not a number is drawn as 0, and when the server does not send the total the three figures are counted on the list and the card says it is a sample. The success rate per repository says the same when its list is a sample.
- **A score ledger that cannot be read is never written over**: when the ledger of the score exists but cannot be read after the retries (Windows, a file held by a backup or an antivirus), the hourly pass does nothing and the problem is noted in the health of the monitor; the last good ledger stays and the next pass reads it again. It used to start from an empty ledger and replace the good one.
- **The report schedule at its edges**: the hour that happens twice when the clock goes back in autumn no longer produces two reports, a monthly report (or a yearly one) that was owed while the monitor was stopped for more than eight days is still written, and a report whose second format cannot be written leaves no file of the first one behind.
- **The log of actions also records what is confirmed on GitHub and on this computer**: a rerun, cancel, dispatch or merge on GitHub (with its action, repository and number), a run with `act`, saving or deleting the stored token, a change of the working mode, a group added by discovery, a lesson or a book of failures added, a report written now, the change of the login of an account and the actions of the AI. Each line has the person, the result and the status, never the body. Every POST route is now either recorded or listed as exempt with its reason (`security/audited_posts.py`) and a test fails when a new route is neither. A silence that could not be created or removed is no longer logged as done, and its line is written once the silence exists.
- **Several processes writing the same file work again on Windows**: the owner of a lock file tries again for about a second to delete it when Windows refuses (a reader had it open), and a lock file younger than a second is no longer opened to read the number of its process (a live owner lets go of it in milliseconds). Before, the lock stayed for 30 seconds while the others gave up after 10, and the writers of the feed, of the log of actions and of the reports failed under load.
- **A lock file left by a writer that died is taken over at once**: the lock file of the file storage holds the number of its process; when that process no longer exists the next writer takes the lock immediately (it used to wait 30 seconds, longer than its own 10-second limit, and failed), and the takeover is logged. When the process cannot be known the age of the file still decides, and that time can be changed with `MONITOR_LOCK_STALE`. A test kills a writer inside the lock and checks that the feed is writable again.
- **Durations and queue times of re-run workflows are no longer absurd**: the wait in the queue and the duration are measured on the latest attempt of a run (`run_attempt`); the creation time of the first try is never used for a re-run, so queues of days and durations of hundreds of days no longer reach the analytics panel, the stuck-run check or the exports. A duration longer than the 35 days a run can last is left empty. The new setting `ci.rerun_timing` (off by default) also asks GitHub for the real duration of finished re-runs.
- **A try after an approval is not "flaky"**: a run that needed a person to approve it (`action_required`) and then passed, and the runs of pull requests that come from forks, no longer count as flaky workflows.
- The failing job of a run with more than 30 jobs is found: the lists of jobs that the alert for an AI, the assistant and the batch tool read were cut at 30 and are now cut at 100 (the same single request).
- **A clock set back no longer stops the score, the purge and the scheduled reports**: a saved moment that lies in the future (a computer without a battery clock that starts with an old date, or a clock corrected by hand) is no longer believed; the pass is due at once and writes a good moment, instead of waiting for days until the old clock is reached again.

## [2.0.2]

### Security

- **One cleaner for everything an AI can read**: the text the integrated assistant sends to a provider, the repository, workflow, link and conclusion of every alert, `GET /api/ai/state`, `GET /api/ai/alerts`, `GET /api/ai/brief?fmt=json`, the feed file, its state file and the body of the hook all go through the same cleaner (before, a password in `DB_PASSWORD=...`, a user and key inside an address, an `sk-` key, a token, an e-mail address, an IP address or the home folder could go out). A test plants a secret of each kind and fails if one shows up in any of those outputs.
- **The integrated assistant keeps its key and stays on this computer**: a call to a provider no longer follows a redirect (the key would have gone on to the other address; the redirect is reported as the answer of the provider) and no longer uses the proxy variables of the environment, so a local server such as Ollama is really called directly. A remote provider goes through the proxy only with `network.use_proxy`.
- **The cleaner knows more forms of a secret**: PGP key blocks, `--password value` and `mysql -phunter2` on a command line, cookies, `Authorization: Basic ...`, `ENCRYPTION_KEY=`, `SENTRY_DSN=` and `AWS_CREDS=`, a value with escaped quotes, a phrase after `password:`, a token cut by a line break, `sk_live_` keys, compressed IPv6 addresses, network shares and the folder of root; text hidden behind an encoding (percent codes, zero-width characters, full-width letters) is cleaned too, and a pair whose key says it is a secret (`{"password": ...}`) is replaced whole. It is a cleaner by pattern: the guide and the skills no longer promise that nothing can get through.
- **Text of third parties is data, not an instruction**: the line that the gate script of an AI tool copies for each alert keeps the repository name only if it is one and cuts the workflow name to a short plain text; a lesson that an AI wrote goes between the delimiters of untrusted data in the prompt of the integrated assistant; a stranger's text (the title of an issue) mentions nobody in Discord and is escaped for Slack.
- **Apprise**: leftover folders with the addresses of the services are swept at the first send, a comma inside an address no longer cuts it, and each address is written quoted.
- **The presets file is validated like the settings page**: only listed fields are served, addresses must be accepted by the page, a cloud preset cannot point to this computer or a private address, and the variable of the key cannot be one of the system or of the monitor (`GH_TOKEN`, `PATH`, `MONITOR_*`...); the same rule applies to the setting `ai.api_key_env`.
- **The process of each account no longer keeps the key of the supervisor and the access token in its environment**: both leave the environment at the very start of every process (monitor, supervisor and account), before the first `gh` or any other program is started, also when the access token has another name. The comment that promised the secrets would be hidden from the process list of Linux now states its real limit.
- **The image can no longer carry the data of whoever builds it**: the build context excluded the loose files of the old layout but not the `config/`, `data/`, `logs/` and `ai/` folders where the tokens, users, sessions and settings live now; they are excluded, the app stage of the Dockerfile deletes them too, and a test checks both against the list of folders of the product.
- **Three visitors with wrong tokens can no longer lock everybody out behind the reverse proxy**: the lock shared by the whole proxy counts only the requests that arrive without a usable `X-Forwarded-For`; a request that carries the address of its visitor is counted against that address alone, the shared lock ends after the lock time and never stops a request that carries the right access token.
- **The time of a login no longer tells which names are users**: a user who is locked costs the same work as a name that does not exist, so "locked" answers in the same time for both.
- **A path under `/api/accounts/` that the supervisor does not filter is judged like any other route**: only the list, the status and the credential of an account skip the account check of the gate, and the credential of an account is refused to a user who cannot see that account.
- **The log of actions keeps one chain when several processes write the same file**: each line is chained to the last line that is really in the file, read under a lock between processes (only the end of the file is read), and that lock also waits when Windows answers "access denied" while another process deletes the lock file.
- **The log of actions is one file for each account**: in the Complete edition with several accounts the processes of different accounts wrote the same `audit_actions.jsonl` (and an administrator of one account could read lines of another); each account now keeps its own file in its folder and `GET /api/audit-log` keeps only the lines of the account that answers. The lines written before stay in the old shared file, which the supervisor still reads.
- **The check of the log of actions covers the rotated file too**: `chain` verifies `audit_actions.jsonl.1` and the link between it and the active file, names the segment (`segment`) when a line is broken, says how many segments it checked (`segments`) and whether the chain begins at its first link (`complete`).
- **The log of actions can no longer be read by a viewer, and it records more of what matters**: the route needs at least the maintainer role at the door (an administrator, or a maintainer when `audit_log.maintainers_can_read` is on, still decides inside), switching the log off writes its own last line, a line that cannot be written is counted and shown (`write_failures`, `last_write_error_at` in `GET /api/audit-log`, and a degradation notice), and failed changes of one's own password stay in the audit of the accesses instead of filling the log of actions.

### Added

- **The notification event `ai_alert`**: a new real alert from a panel or a signal of the monitor can reach the notification channels (add `ai_alert` to `notify.events`; off by default). A red workflow keeps its own event, `red_new`.

### Changed

- **The score now penalizes a failure that is not fixed**: the recovery part counts a workflow that is still red at the end of the window with the time it has been red so far (a lower bound that grows while the red lasts), instead of leaving it out. Before, an account that never fixed its red could score better than one that fixed it in a day. Expect lower numbers for accounts that have a workflow in red when you update. `docs/SCORE.md` explains it in the eight languages.

### Fixed

- **The score and the score ledger**: a re-run of a run is counted as one more execution (GitHub keeps the id, so the first conclusion used to stay for ever); the calculation no longer divides by zero when no part has data (the answer is `insufficient_data` with the reason `no_data`); when the ledger drops runs past 20000 and that reaches the current window the snapshot says so (`data_quality.truncated`); the mean of `GET /api/score/global` leaves out stale snapshots (counted in `accounts_stale`) and `/metrics` does not export `ghamon_score` for them; and the score guide now describes five parts, with `score.weights.security`, in the eight languages.
- **The Security sentinel no longer passes "could not read" for "clean"**: when most of the sources that weigh in the penalty (Dependabot, code scanning, secrets) answer 403 or 404 for every repository, the level is `unknown` instead of `ok` and the security part of the score is left out instead of counting as 100 (the status carries `sources_readable` and `sources_total`). A source that stops answering while an alert is open keeps the alert for a week, marked as unread, and never announces it as fixed; a `gh` that prints nothing is a failed reading, and a default branch with a slash in its name is asked for encoded.
- **A few repositories that always fail no longer starve the Security sentinel**: a repository whose reads fail waits 5 minutes, 30 minutes and then 6 hours before the next try, and the ones not tried yet go first, so the rest of the account is looked at.
- **A repository that the sentinels could not look at is no longer shown as OK in the health of the team**: when every sentinel is in error, paused or degraded (or all are off and nothing has run for a week), a quiet repository is `unknown` ("Not seen"), the ranking rows carry `not_fully_seen` with the names of the sentinels that could not read it, the totals count them, and the Team tab marks the state with an asterisk and a hint. A red workflow or an alert still make it `warn` or `bad`.
- **One damaged byte in the event feed no longer stops the notifications**: past 512 KB the feed was read again as strict UTF-8 to trim it, a single invalid byte raised an error that escaped, the feed grew without a limit and every new event failed before reaching the channels. The trim now reads tolerantly and can never stop the channels.
- **A file in use no longer reads as empty in the files storage**: on Windows a backup, an antivirus or an editor can hold a file for a moment; the reader took the file for empty and the next save wrote on top of it (users, score ledger records, score snapshots, analytics). A file that exists is now read again a few times, and whoever reads to change and save stops with an error instead of overwriting. Several processes appending to the same event feed take a lock between processes, so no line is lost or cut on Windows.
- **Repeating `migrate_storage` no longer replaces newer data in the target**: an account whose target has newer events or snapshots is reported as `target newer`, refused without writing anything, and the exit code is 1; `--force` makes the source win. The run also prints a warning with the count of damaged lines of the event feed that it cannot copy (the source keeps them).
- **A backup is checked completely before a restore writes anything**: an account id or a name that is not valid anywhere in the copy makes `verify` fail, so a restore can no longer stop half way with the first accounts already written.
- **An answer of the MCP server that is longer than its limit is still a valid document**: rows are left out of the longest list instead of cutting the text, the answer says `truncated` and how many rows are missing, and the health signals (old data, degraded monitor, allowance) are the last to go.
- **A number that is not a number no longer cuts the connection**: `?since=`, `?limit=` and `Last-Event-ID` accept only the digits 0-9 (and at most 15); anything else is the default.
- **The state of the scheduled reports is shared safely by several accounts**: each account changes its entry under a lock, and a replace that a reader blocks for a moment on Windows is tried again.
- **The tables of the skills say which calls are only for this computer**: the table is generated from the same lists the server uses, and `/api/ai/failures` lists its POST. A report no longer carries raw titles: the titles of alerts go through the cleaner and Markdown does not turn an address into a link by itself.
- **The API contract now covers `/api/data` and cannot be trimmed**: the route that gives the runs, the repositories and the allowance was not in the frozen contract. It is now, the routes that are readable but not frozen are listed under `unfrozen` with their reason, a test fails when a new GET route is in neither list, and another compares the contract with the one of the first 2.x version so that a path, a key or a type cannot disappear in the same commit that breaks the API.
- **The window of a repository opened from the Team tab showed nothing**: a row of the ranking carries only the name, and the page asked the health of the repository with an empty owner; it now uses the owner of the page. The deep link `?view=repo&repo=owner/name` also no longer fills the text filter of the Now tab with the repository, which left that tab empty.
- **A workflow, job or repository name with brackets no longer breaks the terminal views**: `test [ubuntu, 3.13]` was shown as `test`, `docs [/api]` ended the view with an error, and a name could carry styles or links of the terminal. The names are drawn as plain text in both views, and a picture that cannot be drawn is skipped instead of ending the view.
- **An empty Enter at the menu of the Windows batch launcher repeated the last option**: choosing 2 and pressing Enter again fired the whole batch a second time, because `set /p` keeps the old value when nothing is typed. The variable is cleared before each question, and a test runs the real launcher.
- **Scripts with a shebang had Windows line endings in the published zip**: the 33 `.py` files that start with `#!` (the entry points of `bin/` among them) could not be run directly on Linux or macOS when the zip was built on Windows. The zip now carries them with LF and the release gate fails if one still has a CR in its first line.

## [2.0.1]

### Fixed

- **The published zip lacked the AI presets file**: the packaging now includes every data file of the package and the release gate fails if one is missing from the zip.

- **An untranslated state key on the all-accounts page**: the two missing keys are now in the eight catalogues, and a test checks that every `ui` key used by the page exists in the eight catalogues.

## [2.0.0]

### Added

- **The contract of the read API, frozen from here on**: `docs/schemas/api_contract.json` lists the 37 read endpoints with the keys and the types of their answers in each edition (and which of them the Lite edition answers with `404`) and the error codes of the door (`401 access`, `403 forbidden`, `404`). A key listed there keeps its name and its type for the whole of 2.x; new keys may appear and nothing is removed without a new major version. The suite `test_200_contract.py` checks it against a real server of each edition and also the rules of the door: a foreign `Host` is `403`, the network mode asks for the token in the header `X-Monitor-Access` and answers `401 access` without it, the words of a query that nobody reads are ignored and every answer is JSON in UTF-8.

- **A tool to check any running monitor against the contract**: `python tools/check_api_contract.py` (`--lite`, `--url`, `--token-file`) reads the contract, asks the monitor with `GET` only, prints one line for each key that is missing or has another type and ends with `CONTRACT=PASS` or `CONTRACT=FAIL` (exit code 0, 1, or 2 when nobody answers). It is for the people whose scripts, wall screens or mobile clients depend on the answers, after each upgrade of the monitor or of the client. It makes no call to GitHub.

- **The interfaces for sentinels and storages are frozen by a test too**: `test_200_interfaces.py` fixes the nine fields of an `Alert`, the methods of `BaseSentinel` and of `Storage` with the names of their parameters, the kinds of record, and that both storages and the three sentinels implement every method. A change there is a change of contract.

- **One guide for each use, in the eight languages**: Complete for one person (`COMPLETE_INDIVIDUAL.md`), Complete as a service (`COMPLETE_SERVICE.md`), Complete for a team (`COMPLETE_TEAM.md`) and the API for clients (`API.md`), next to the short guide of the Lite edition. They are in the Help panel, in the list of guides of the README, and a test checks that they exist in the eight languages, that their links resolve and that every setting and endpoint they name exists. A second test reads every guide of the documentation in the eight languages and fails when one names a route that the code does not have.

### Changed

- **Version 2.0.0, the monitor is declared mature**: from now on the read endpoints, the `Alert`, `BaseSentinel` and `Storage` change only with a new major version (an addition that old clients can ignore is allowed and noted here). The message of the product is the same in the README, the guides and the help: Lite is the simple edition for one person on one computer, Complete is the control centre (several accounts, a team, a service and clients). Nothing else in the behaviour of the monitor changes in this version.

## [1.9.5]

### Added

- **The live events as a stream (Complete, off by default)**: with `live.sse.enabled` on, `GET /api/live-events/stream` sends the events of the incoming webhook as they are accepted, as server-sent events: a `hello` frame (how many are held), one `live` frame per event with a running `id` (resume with `Last-Event-ID` or `?since=`; only the closed list of fields of `GET /api/live-events` is ever sent), a keep-alive comment and a final `bye`. It is read only, asks for the access token in the network mode like any other request, keeps at most `live.sse.max_clients` listeners (more get `429`) each for `live.sse.max_seconds`, and the Lite edition does not have it. The three settings are in the Settings tab (sub-tab Incoming webhook) with their help in the eight languages, the guide of the incoming webhook explains it, and the references of the two skills list the endpoint. A test with a real server covers off by default, the limit of listeners and an accepted event reaching the listener.

## [1.9.4]

### Added

- **Presets of AI providers and a table of compatible AIs**: a versioned file of data (`ai_presets.json`, served read only at `GET /api/ai/presets`) lists twelve hosted services (OpenAI, Anthropic, Google Gemini, xAI Grok, Mistral, DeepSeek, Groq, OpenRouter, Together AI, Fireworks AI, Perplexity, Cohere) and six local servers (Ollama, LM Studio, llama.cpp server, vLLM, LocalAI, Jan) with editable example models (Llama, Mistral, Qwen, DeepSeek, Gemma, Phi, gpt-oss; no weights, no downloads). In the AI settings a selector fills the provider, the address, the NAME of the environment variable of the key and a model, shows the privacy of the choice (text sent over the internet or kept on this computer) and says "compatible by protocol, not tested with this tool" where it is so; none is active by default and the key never leaves its environment variable. A server on this computer (`http://127.0.0.1:...`) may now be saved as `ai.base_url` and may work without a key; a remote address still needs https and its key. The three AI guides carry the table in the eight languages, and a fake server with the OpenAI protocol tests the answers 200, 401, 429 and a timeout, with the key absent from every output.

## [1.9.3]

### Added

- **Apprise as a channel of notifications (Complete, off by default)**: `notify.channels.apprise` hands the message to the `apprise` command, if it is installed, so that any service Apprise knows can be reached. The addresses (they hold the secrets of those services) are read from the environment variable named by `urls_env`, written to a private temporary file for the instant of the call and removed, never put in the arguments, and the command gets an environment without the keys of the monitor. Apprise opens its own connections, so the guard of the monitor on private addresses does not apply to it: the channel has its own switch, marked as a security setting. A missing command is the reason `not_installed`, a failure is counted and never stops the monitor.
- **A log of actions (Complete only, on by default)**: next to the audit of the accesses, `logs/audit_actions.jsonl` keeps one JSON line for each change (who, what, when, on which account): a change of settings (the names of the settings, never their values), a silence and its removal, a change of users, passwords, notification preferences or credentials. Each line carries the hash of the one before it, so a line that is changed, cut out or put in later is found (`GET /api/audit-log` says where the chain breaks); past `audit_log.max_mb` the old part is kept as one more file and the chain goes on. No value and no secret is ever written (a key that looks like a secret is dropped, every text is cleaned and cut). An administrator reads it, and a maintainer only when `audit_log.maintainers_can_read` is on. Three settings in the Sentinel tab and the guide AUDIT_LOG.md in the eight languages.

- **Scheduled reports (Complete only, off by default)**: the monitor writes a report of the account on a cron schedule (`report_schedule.cron`, five fields in the local time, `0 8 * * 1` by default) or when an administrator asks at this computer: the score (no number while it is calibrating or has too little data), what changed since the last report, the repositories that need a person and the open alerts, as one HTML page with a print style (a PDF is the print-to-PDF of the browser) and as Markdown. It is made from what the monitor already holds, asks GitHub for nothing and sends nothing: the files stay in `data/reports/`, the oldest runs go after `report_schedule.keep`, a report is written once for each scheduled minute (a long stop gives one, not a pile), the first one waits for the first scheduled minute after the switch-on, a failure is noted and tried again at the next one, and every text from the data is escaped. The Team tab lists the files and has the button "Write a report now"; `GET /api/report-files`, `GET /api/report-files/file?name=` (only a file the service wrote for the account) and `POST /api/report-files/run` are the routes. Five settings in the Sentinel tab, the guide REPORT_SCHEDULE.md in the eight languages.
- **A harness and the reproductions of the independent review, in the repository**: `tools/audit_harness.py --check` starts, with no GitHub, a supervisor with three accounts (alpha, beta, default) and four users (administrator, maintainer of alpha only, viewer of beta only, one switched off), asks the routes that decide the isolation of the accounts and prints what each user gets; `tests/test_193_regressions.py` holds the reproductions of the review of 1.8.3 (accounts of a request, accounts kept when a password changes, silences of a remote maintainer, the test message and its hourly limit, a restored copy and revoked sessions, the lock and the administrator, the keys a started program does not inherit).

### Changed

- **A test no longer reads a file of the person who wrote it**: the check against the real file of the profile repository runs only when `MONITOR_REAL_CENTRAL_FILE` says where it is.

## [1.9.2]

### Added

- **The health of each repository and of the team (Complete only)**: from what the monitor already holds (runs, red and stuck workflows, the open alerts of the three sentinels and the readiness of the releases) a state is worked out for each repository (bad, warning, OK or not seen) with a risk number; a repository that no sentinel looked at, or that one could not read, never comes out as OK and says who did not see it fully. `GET /api/repos/<owner>/<repo>/health` gives the detail and `GET /api/team/health` the ranking, the totals, the numbers by ecosystem and by sentinel and the filters (ecosystem, least severity, a piece of the name). They never call GitHub.
- **The Team tab and the detail of a repository**: the ranking with the repositories that need a person first, three filters, and a window with the CI, the alerts, the release and the activity when a repository is clicked; the deep links `?view=team`, `?view=repos` and `?view=repo&repo=owner/name` (with `chrome=0` for wall screens) open each view. The Lite edition does not have it.
- **The `team` command**: `monitor_cli.py team` prints the ranking (`--severity warn|bad`) and `team --repo owner/name` the detail of one, with `--json`; read only and from the same routes as the page. The guide HEALTH.md, in the eight languages, explains it all.

## [1.9.1]

### Added

- **Security is a part of the score**: the points that the open findings of the Security sentinel take off 100 (a critical alert, a high one, a secret, a branch with no protection, workflows with write permission) are the fifth part, with the weight `score.weights.security` (25 by default) and its own limits; it only counts while the sentinel is on and has looked at enough repositories, and without it the answer says that the number is not complete and the other parts are rescaled. The security figures are the same in the window and in the one before it, because GitHub gives no history of them.

### Changed

- **The default weights of the score are now 30, 15, 15, 15 and 25** (reliability, speed, recovery, collaboration, security) so that the fifth part has room; they are settings, as before.
